PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. You have the right to do so. PALMA (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, this morning the Commission received a note from the head of the Department of Penitentiary Administration dealing with the data repeatedly requested and which until yesterday were defined as particularly articulated and difficult to transmit. I will leave copies available to the Presidency for the senators who would like to consult them. The data transmitted by the Department reveals the following: first of all, at 31 March 2011, the prison population was 67,600 and not 70,000, as rounded up by the Undersecretary in yesterday's response. In any case, as regards the measures of interest, at the entry into force of Decree-Law No. 78 of 2013 - so we are in June 2013 - the prison population was 66,028 units. Six months after the entry into force of that measure, the prison population was 62,536, which is equivalent to saying that that decree, with the modification made to the Senate of raising the roofs of the protective custody (of course, President, the data are what they are, so we go to waste), has resulted in an impact on the prison population of about 3,500 units. Well, on December 23, 2012, the decree-law that we are discussing comes into force. At the time the prison population was 62,536; to date, almost two months after the date of entry into force of the measure, the prison population is 61,225. Therefore, in these two months the decree-law that we are going to discuss today has resulted in an impact on the prison population of just 1,200 units, placing the prison presence at more than 13,000 units above the 48,000 places available, so far above what Europe requires. It is clear that the list of data makes me well aware of the reason why I insisted on the need to know the data, also in the light - as then will be seen in the debate on the amendments - of the impact on the system of prison execution that the measure we are going to discuss has had, has and will have.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u13	Ladies and gentlemen, yesterday morning the Presidency received the request, made by the prescribed number of senators, to carry out by secret ballot the deliberations relating to 300 amendments and the final vote of the measure in our examination. I would remind you that, recalling the established practice, the Presidency, at its meetings of 24 March 2009, n. 178, and 19 January 2010, n. 314, reiterated that, pursuant to Rule 113 of the Rules of Procedure, the Assembly would normally vote by show of hands, and in any case clearly. In particular, the regulatory amendment adopted in 1988 was aimed, as is apparent from the preparatory work itself, at restricting the scope of secret voting to strictly and strictly determined cases. In view of the above principles, the Presidency considered that it would confirm the practice under which secret ballot is not admissible for all deliberations concerning in a general way the rights of freedom relating to civil and ethical-social relations provided for in the articles of the Constitution referred to in Article 113 (4) of the Rules of Procedure, but exclusively for those which have the effect of restricting or restricting the rights themselves. Similarly, the Presidency has made it clear that requests for secret ballots are not admissible for types of amendments which can be attributed, either exclusively or predominantly, to the definition of procedural cases relating to the principles of the fair trial, referred to in Article 111 of the Constitution. At its meeting on 25 January 2012, No 664, the Presidency did not deviate from the settled case-law and considered the secret ballot for matters with purely procedural content inadmissible. This last precedent is of particular significance, not only because it confirms the principles of regulation and practice, but also because the measure before the Assembly concerned urgent action to combat the detention tension caused by the overcrowding of prisons. Consequently, the Presidency, pursuant to Rule 113 (7) of the Rules of Procedure, cannot accept the request for secret ballot for the final vote of the measure, either on the basis of the last precedent of the measure entirely "in terms" or in the light of the criterion of prevalence which requires a qualitative and quantitative assessment of the rules that do not fall within the matters referred to in Article 113 (4) of the Rules of Procedure. As regards the request for a secret vote on specific amendments, the Presidency considers it acceptable for those listed on the list which is being distributed and which I hope has already been distributed. On the other hand, the request for a secret vote for the following amendments is not acceptable: 1,200, as it has a discipline less afflictive than the judgment of application of the sentence on request; 1.8, 1.9, 1.10, 1.11, 1.12 and 1.13, as they affect points (b) and (c) of paragraph 1, which extend the use of the simplified procedure in the matter of execution of the sentence and therefore have a procedural nature; 1.14, 1.15, 1.16 and 1.37, which affect instead the paragraph 2 of Article 1, relating to the entry into force of the provisions referred to in paragraph 1; 1.0,200 and 1.0.201, since, aimed at regulating the activities to be carried out during the stay in prison, and in particular the work and training of prisoners, introduce less affluent rules, and therefore do not have a restrictive effect on personal freedom; 2.30, which contains a provision for coordination with respect to the introduction of the new crime of the so-called "small store"; 2.39, introducing measures to simplify access to the therapeutic use of cannabis; 3.4a, which introduces a formal amendment to the wording of the Article; 3.20, 3.21, 3.22, 3.23, 3.25, 3.26, 3.26, 3.31, 3.32, 3.33, 3.35, 3.35, 3.36, 3.37, 3.38, 3.39, 3.41, 3.42, 3.43, 3.44 and 3.47, as it intervenes on procedural provisions relating to the complaint of the prisoners; 3.67 and 3.68, which affect procedural provisions relating to jurisdiction in the field of probation; 3.79, 3.80, 3.81, 3.82, 3.83, 3.84, 3.85, 3.86, 3.89, 3.90, 3.91, 3.93, 3.93, 3.94, 3.95, 3.96, 3.97, 3.98, 3.99, 3.100, 3.101 and 3.102, which concern the rules for the entry into force of the provisions referred to in Article 3; 4.7, 4.138, 4.139, 4.141 and 4.142, which do not relate to the nature of the identification of the application of the institute of the special early release; 6.2, since the Commission voted on it, in accordance with paragraph 1 of Article 6 (1) of the Constitution, which could not be affected by the merely;

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u22	I therefore ask, of course, with the support of my group's colleagues, that amendments 7,200 and 7,201 should also be put to the vote in secret. This is the reason for this request in very simple terms. Article 7 of the decree-law allows the Guarantor for prisoners to visit without authorization also private facilities where an alternative measure is discounted to protective custody in prison. Let me remind you that Article 14 of our Constitution declares the inviolability of the domicile, and allows access to the domicile in case of dissent of those who have the availability of the private place only after authorization of the judicial authority or in cases of flagrantness for what concerted the action of the judicial police. My two amendments tend precisely to exclude private structures from the places that the Inmates' Supervisor can visit without any authorisation, or, in the event that this amendment is rejected, to allow this authorisation to take place with the consent of the owner of the private structure. In other cases, Mr President (but we will discuss this when we get there), there would be a clear violation of Article 14 of the Constitution from my personal point of view. Despite the many years of experience, I do not know whether colleagues' support is required from the procedural point of view. If necessary, I would ask my fellow Members to support the request for secret voting for amendments 7,200 and 7,201, except for the assessments that the Presidency will make on this matter.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u41	Mr President, I rise to explain my amendments to Article 1. The European Court of Human Rights condemns Italy for its prison system that violates the rights of prisoners. In our prisons there are conditions at the limit of legal imprisonment. Can a human be living in less than 3 square meters? The solution for the government and for this frigolian and cat pardian majority is simple: all out. The 5 Star Movement does not exist: this provision is offensive to honest citizens and to a rule of law that recognizes full citizenship to the certainty of punishment and its ethical value. A certain penalty, as our legal tradition teaches, from Beccaria onwards, and as enshrined in the Constitution, must however be human, taken for granted in respect of the dignity of the individual, for whom the limitation of freedom must represent not only a moment of atonement for errors, but above all an important opportunity of re-education and reconciliation with civil society. Our amendment aims precisely to reaffirm the principles set out: to reconfirm and translate into reality the idea that the inmate's stay in prisons must be safe and respectful of the sanitary standards and standard ergonomic criteria for the living spaces of the general population. This, however, is only the premise of a theorem of reeducation and personal growth that finds its cornerstones in training and especially in work, understood as law, obligation and decisive passage for an effective and not abstract reintegration into society. All this, however, costs society, which is why it is appropriate that the remuneration linked to the work carried out during the period of detention should flow 90% into the coffers of the State, under the heading "Sources for the maintenance expenses of prisoners". The atonement of the sentence and the re-education of the person must not represent, today more than ever, a social cost imposed on the pockets of the community, but must be demanded by the same subject who has ignored the rules of civil coexistence. [[Applause from Senator Casaletto.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u74	Mr President, the rule adopted deleting paragraph 5 of Article 94 of the decree of the President of the Republic No 309 of 9 October 1990 is serious, and I would ask you to pay attention. It is a serious rule, because Article 94 regulates the assumption of probation in special cases for drug addicts and provides that, in the case of a six-year imprisonment sentence (I do not have to explain what are the offences for a six-year imprisonment sentence), or four-year imprisonment for terrorist offences, massacres and so on, the drug addict, if he is being treated therapeutically, discounts that penalty in probation - as you all know - with the end of the sentence. Paragraph 5 of Article 94 stipulated that it was not possible to rely on probation for more than twice. Beware that we are faced with the possibility of twelve years of imprisonment or ten years of imprisonment or eight years of imprisonment, in the case of two terrorist offences. This limit had a double value: on the one hand, it induced the drug addict to continue the therapeutic treatment program; on the other, it created a deterrent to commit further crimes, already knowing that, however, for different common crimes, these people can enjoy, without limits, custody in evidence. As far as this rule is concerned, those who have a minimum of experience or knowledge of the phenomenon of drug addiction or criminal trials know that there is rarely a convicted person in these conditions who has been sentenced more than twice at that level and who has taken advantage of custody in evidence for both times. Then I wonder: why alter a criminal system for a benefit that does not affect the number of prisoners? We have already reduced it to 61,000 prisoners, in 2009 there were 69,000, and we have endured and spread a prison management over these years with a series of rules that we have helped to vote. But this means affecting the criminal system without any consequence on the prison system. I wonder what criterion we are voting on: just so that a correct decree is not passed or passed by the House? I think it is really crazy to lower the need to defend the community at this level. For what? We don't even have an influence on the prison situation. So what's the point? It only serves not to give concrete answers to the phenomenon of increasingly heinous crime.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u78	Mr President, I rise to explain the agenda for G2,200, which I very much care about, because I believe it can help and have positive consequences for the crowding of prisons in our country. I would start from the idea that cannabis, with reference to some of its active principles, has proven its effectiveness in various therapeutic areas, both in the treatment of pain and muscle spasticity in multiple sclerosis, as well as nausea and vomiting following chemotherapy, but also in the deperimento syndrome in AIDS and neuropathic pain. Therefore, the use of cannabis is proven to have therapeutic effects. Although the therapeutic use of cannabis derivatives has been authorised since 1990 by decree of the President of the Republic n. 309, there are still no legal sources of supply for cannabis in Italy. In our country there are no medicines registered with cannabinoids and there are no authorized manufacturers, while the prescription and import of a medicinal product authorised in another country is allowed. Finally, there are many legislative measures at regional level that demonstrate the increase in attention to the therapeutic use of cannabis. Furthermore, on 12 February 2014, the Constitutional Court declared illegal, for violation of Article 77, second paragraph, of the Constitution, the articles in which the drug law was amended, eliminating for sanctions the distinction between heavy drugs and light drugs and providing for an increase in penalties. Although the ruling of the Constitutional Court does not enter into the legitimacy of the equivalence between heavy drugs and light drugs, it remains the incontrovertible fact that this judgment has effects, requiring regulatory action that regulates the matter in a comprehensive way. For this reason we propose to commit the government to give a signal, to confront the regulation of the use of cannabis without prejudice starting from its use for therapeutic purposes and to note that certain measures produce, among other things, the beneficial side effect of lightening the prison population and giving new spaces to a productive sector that at a time such as this should not be ignored. Article 77 speaks only of necessity and urgency, whereas homogeneity is a criterion which the Court subsequently introduced. So the practical effect of the Court's ruling is that those who have been released will have to go back to prison.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u110	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u111	Gasparri (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, on my own behalf, I would like to say that I will not be voting on this amendment, because I want the situation of contradiction in which we find ourselves to remain in the record. The sentence limit (which was previously four years old) that allowed drug addicts to be released from prison had been raised to six years. We are talking about the problem of prison overcrowding and the problems that pertain to this provision and we forget that there is a rule, not sufficiently applied in our country. This rule allows, for sentences up to six years, not to be in prison and to stay elsewhere, that is in community, in SERT, in places where a recovery path outside the prison allows the addict to have a better condition than that prison, with all the controversy that there are also compared to the fact that the drug circulates in prison. The judgment of the Court (I write it to the Parliament as well as to the Government, indeed to the Governments, and I use the plural to refer to outgoings and enterings), which still has to be evaluated because it has not been published (but its contents have emerged), means that, for that cancellation concerning the procedure by which it has legislated (not the merit, that is, if the decree in the conversion could be modified in a certain way), that benefit is reduced to sentences up to four years, and not up to six years. We are here introducing a measure that allows the possibility of going to community for sentences up to six years, when the sentence, which is still not published but already issued, cancels this rule. There's a little bit of crazy legislation. That is why I will not take part in the vote on this amendment. We are talking about a prediction that the judgment of the Court, in my opinion mistaken, reduces. That benefit had been brought up to six years precisely because the communities and operators believed that it was a form of solidarity, tense hand and decarceration, to use a bad term (but now this is also used). We are in a complete contradiction between judgments, decrees and reality, so I do not want to endorse this confusion with an unnecessary vote.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u116	CALIENDO (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, the rewording of amendment 2.3, tabled by Senator Airola and other senators, prevents the Senate from intervening with a rule that makes it difficult to interpret and evaluate the effects of the succession of criminal laws over time. After the decision of the Constitutional Court, the law of Jervolino-Vassalli revives and the amendment, in the second version resulting from the reformulation, corresponds to the norm of the law of Jervolino-Vassalli. At this point, then, voting against this amendment means introducing a new discipline that still maintains the uniqueness of the sanction, without distinction, for offences related to soft drugs and those related to heavy drugs, and not taking into account the consequences foreshadowed in the judgment of the Constitutional Court, which we can, at least for the device, already take as certain, without having to wait for its publication. Undersecretary Berretta said that the logic of this rule lies in creating a deterrent against the adults who use minors for dealing. The deterrent would be to exclude from the calculation of the quantification of the sentence for minors the decrease of the minor age in order to allow the issue of non-convict custody against minors. The Secretary also stated in his reply that it was a non-convict precautionary measure and that there was no possibility of a prison precautionary measure. Well, I want to represent the Assembly... President. I call on you to conclude on the subject because time is over; you have one minute at your disposal, which has already passed. I'll give you another minute, Senator Palma. PALMA (FI-PdL XVII). I would like to put to the House the reason why children who commit the crime of small drug dealing should be excluded from premium treatment, whereas, for example, children who participate in association with criminals, children who participate in the fight aggravated even by the dead, children who are responsible for ill-treatment in the family are not. The second paragraph of Article 31 of the Constitution, which imposes premium legislation on minors, is also silent on the legislator. Finally, I would like to say one last thing: I am sorry, Mr Berretta, but perhaps in his reply he forgot to say that, when a non-convict precautionary measure is issued against minors, in the background there is the prison precautionary measure - I believe in Article 20 or 21 - whenever the child violates the requirements imposed on custody. This is a clearly unconstitutional rule, which is a criminal policy requirement for minors and which I really do not think can be put into effect here. Vote by secret ballot

I am not saying this on a political issue: since six months of the elimination of the sentence for the three years already gone by will be guaranteed, it is nothing more than an indult. There's no re-evaluation. As the Forza Italia Group, we have raised the problem, and we have also tabled amendments in this House which allowed for an increase in the deduction of sentences with a special early release of up to sixty days, instead of forty-five. We had argued that there are aspects of unconstitutionality. Mr President, house arrest does not apply to those under house arrest. This senate, just a month ago, equalized house arrest to prison detention. You teach me that early release does not take account of the type of crime, but only takes into account the way in which you conduct yourself during the execution of the sentence. At the time, there was no position against disregarding and ignoring the requirements of the prison system; however, it was preferred not to discuss. Mr President, this is probably also the responsibility of the Conference of Leaders, because we asked for a debate next week. Small corrections would have been sufficient to bring this rule into line with the Constitution and the general principles of the law, to make it applicable to all. So, the amendment... ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u142	CALIENDO (FI-PdL XVII). I have tabled a deleterious amendment to the article, but on the individual questions of the 60 days and house arrests there are appropriate amendments.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u143	Mr President, what we can say in one minute is that the statement made by Mr Caliendo is a very good one. In fact, the institute already exists, the forty-five days per semester are already planned; to increase by another thirty days means to reach seventy-five days per semester, ergo one hundred and fifty days per year: it means that it is deliberated that one year of imprisonment corresponds, in fact, to seven months. We can say that, in the end, this represents an indult camouflage. Therefore, it is not possible, with a decree-law, to establish an early release or a discount of amnesty or indult-type punishment, also because the constitutional rule, in Article 79, establishes exactly how an indult must be operated: first there is a reserve of law, special quorums are needed and, in any case, it must be voted by the two branches of Parliament (it is therefore not a matter of governmental competence). Article 4 should therefore be rejected as it could also be challenged for inconstitutionality.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u146	Mr President, I would like to explain Amendment 4.9, which provides for the exclusion of the benefit of the seventy-five-day penalty discount every one hundred and eighty days of detention for those convicted of corruption, concussion and, of course, corruption in judicial proceedings. Mr President, only a few days ago the humiliating report by the European Union which gives our country the primacy of corruption in Europe passed. Corruption is a cancer that takes away at least 60 billion a year. What do we do? Do we finally implement international conventions against corruption, or do we tighten the penalties, or do we lengthen the prescription, or do we make the current law more effective? No, we are doing exactly the opposite: we are extending the benefit of over 40% penalty discount even to those convicted of corruption and concussion. If we reject this amendment, inmates for corruption will be able to count, as well as on a colabrodo legislation, which makes go to jail with the dropper, also on this incredible penalty discount. Once again, the message we are sending to the country is the opposite of what it should be. In this way we are inciting corruption, rather than suppressing it in a struggle without a neighborhood to eradicate it from our country. Yet, in the Justice Commission, the debate on the new law on corruption - which for a long time has been declared divisive for the balance of this Government - has already begun and is moving towards an extension of the crime cases and a tightening of the penalties: those same penalties that we are now reducing by 40%. I know that many of you - and I am addressing you in particular to my colleagues in the Democratic Party Group - have already signed the petition "Restarting the future" for a new and more effective anti-corruption law. You have signed to ensure legal certainty and to build citizens' confidence in those who represent the institutions. You have signed a commitment and now you are doing exactly the opposite. If you pass this umpteenth gift to corrupt and corrupt, I invite you to go and explain it to the citizens. [[Applause from the M5S Group. Comments from the PD Group]]

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u170	BUCCARELLA (M5S). Mr President, we are asking for the utmost attention, despite the fact that we have not been asked for a secret vote on this vote, and we hope that those who have not done so will not regret it. It is important to draw your attention to this detail: as formulated in paragraph 1 of Article 4, despite what can be thought and what has been said, it will not prevent the judiciary from granting the benefit of special early freedom also to those sentenced for one of the offences referred to in article 4-a of Law n. 354 of 1975, which, let us remember, are mafia, exversion, terrorism and other serious crimes. This is because, in the literal wording, to say "excluding the condemned" does not mean anything, at a time when a mobster is currently serving a sentence relating to satellite crimes. That prisoner will enjoy the benefit, despite the original executive title (this is why our amendment) included a sentence under Article 4-a, perhaps for robbery, extortion and misuse of weapons. If this decree is destined to pass without any change (and the majority will succeed in making it pass), be prepared to read in the newspapers, in the coming weeks and months, reports of mobsters who will be released in advance for having enjoyed this benefit. Attention: the responsibility is in your hands, in a clear way. Let's not let that happen, because the Italians won't forgive us. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] . Nominal vote by simultaneous ballot

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u174	PALMA (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, I would like to thank Senator Malan for not having insisted on the request for a secret vote, so that, in a very open manner, everyone will assume responsibility for the vote that he is going to vote on. The problem is very simple. According to the article that comes from the House, the 75-day discount cannot be applied to convicted persons for the offences referred to in Article 4-a of Law No 354 of 1975. Among the crimes referred to in Article 4-a, there is no Article 416-b of the Criminal Code, which we launched a few days ago in this House, making us all aware of how important it is to have an impact on the complicity between organised crime and politics. I wonder why those seventy-five days which cannot be given to the Mafia can be granted to persons convicted of the offence referred to in Article 416-b, i.e. for political-mafia electoral exchange, the penalty of which is absolutely identical (awaiting the text that was launched here in the Senate) to the penalty provided for in Article 416- bis of the Criminal Code. It can be said that it is possible to make a correction on the point, thus recognizing that there has been an error on the part of the House. But this error could have been calmly healed here in the Senate, given the perfect bicameralism, where the timing of the provision had been different. In other words, those of you who are voting against this amendment will vote in favour of granting the 75-day premium to all those convicted of the crime referred to in Article 416-b of the Criminal Code.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u175	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u176	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u177	LUMIA (PD). Mr President, it is up to me to speak on this point, which, as President Palma has addressed, can create a certain disorientation in this House. First of all, I would like to point out to all the members of the Assembly that, on Monday, in the preliminary question on the constitutionality of the decree, from several parts, both by the 5 Star Movement and by Forza Italia, it was pointed out that it had been inserted by the Chamber, as corrected by the decree of the Government, the exclusion of certain particular crimes of serious social alarm, included in article 4-a of the penitentiary order. And it was said that this insertion, since it could discriminate between crime and crime, was a reason for unconstitutionality. We have argued the opposite, because in our system the issue of double track (great invention of Judge Falcone) has been legitimized several times by the Court in constitutional jurisprudence. I refer in particular to Article 41-a. So, from this point of view, we consistently said that it was constitutional and that the inclusion of Article 4-a was appropriate. I would like to refer to all the components of Article 4-a: "delicts committed for purposes of terrorism, including international terrorism, or for the exversion of the democratic order (...), a crime referred to in Article 416-a of the Criminal Code" (Mafia association). I would also like to draw the attention of all the Members of this House to the observation that President Palma has made to us, in order to point out that we were prepared, if time had been sufficient, to make a better specification. But I want it to remain in the records, when reading the formation of the law, that here we are also covered with 416-b, because we refer to 'delicts committed using the conditions provided for in the same article or in order to facilitate the activities of the associations provided for in it. This article also includes the crimes referred to in articles of the Criminal Code 600 (reduction or maintenance in slavery or servitude), 600-a (child prostitution), 600-b (child pornography), 601 (treatment of persons), Article 602 (purchase or alienation of slaves), 609 (group sexual violence), 630 (sequestration of person for robbery or extortion). And then, again, Article 291-c (association for criminals aimed at smuggling manufactured tobacco) of the single text in customs matters and, with reference to the decree of the President of the Republic n. 309 of 1990, Article 74 (association aimed at illicit drug trafficking). This transitional regime lasts two years, so it has a special character. I would therefore like to point out the contradiction between those who, in the debate, argued that this double track is unconstitutional and those who, today, on the subject, believe that further crimes should be included in that double track context which has been planned for the House and which we support. We can therefore vote on the rule; there is this possibility also referred to Article 416-b, so that in the course of further intervention we can ensure that these changes of substance, which contradict what is proposed to the House, can be better recovered and transposed by the legislator.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u216	CASSON (PD). Mr President, I am speaking on the agenda for G5100, which we have tabled as a PD Group. PRESIDENT. - The debate is It is clear to us that the G5100 agenda has been withdrawn (I must say that they did not warn you), perhaps because it has already been accepted in the Commission. CASSON (PD). It seems to me that a small change has been made, even in relation to signatures. I would ask you, however, to allow me to speak for a few seconds, because it represents structural needs. The measure before us, as we have received from the House of Deputies in the last few days, just before the deadline, would certainly have needed some changes, such as rules of order, but also of a structural type. In particular, the G5.100 agenda, which has already been adopted, recalls the needs of external surveillance and enforcement offices. In short, they are social workers. There is a considerable increase in the workload and responsibility for these offices, and it is natural, indeed obvious, that every reform is proceeding if the structures that must be carried out are functioning. These are delicate and difficult tasks, because they relate to the social reintegration of those sentenced, to the recovery of legality and to the guarantee of social security. In this respect, we stress the importance of this structural indication, so that the Government undertakes - as it did in fact in the Commission - to provide structurally for the needs of the external surveillance and criminal enforcement offices.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u224	Mr President, I would like to point out that, with regard to amendment 6.5, I have taken into account what Senator Sacconi, President of the NCD Group, has said in relation to the issue of the elimination of the crime of illegality. The rapporteur and the government have given assurances, both to President Sacconi and to myself, but also to the whole Assembly, that Article 13 would not be amended, namely that, following a conviction, the foreigner will be expelled after atonement in our country. In the rule under consideration, however, immediate expulsion is allowed, without being tried, even though it has already been condemned and expelled from our country. I believe that this is something abnormal, Mr President, and I believe that you yourself should guarantee the dignity of this House, because if the rapporteur and the government guarantee that a rule will not be touched and that it is being touched today, something probably does not work.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u225	The government changes, Senator Caliendo! CALIENDO (FI-PdL XVII). It is not a matter of change: the Senate voted on the basis of a guarantee, just twenty days ago, which now fails. With regard to amendment 6.6, it is possible, in the event of armed robbery in the apartment or in the shop and in the event of aggravated extortion, to expel the foreigner without any penalty being granted. This is very serious, especially in the light of the fact that in recent months there has been a striking arrival of illegal foreigners in our country - and the Government should know this - and also, as emerges from all the reports of the prosecutors general, there has been an increase in the robberies in the apartments and in the shops and thefts in the apartment. That is the situation, and we are going to say that the perpetrators are not being punished, but are only expected to be expelled. And we all know - just have a minimum of experience - that expulsions have no effect, because there is no accompaniment.

Mr President, when we dealt with the provision on alternative measures, the President of the New Right Centre Group basically gave the floor to the measure provided that foreigners who are expelled with accompanying punishment did not have a different penalty than that of protective custody (i.e. in other words, that their crime was not decriminalised). Because of this agreement, which I imagine has been reached in the government, the New Right Center voted on that measure. I should like to ask the friends and colleagues of the New Right Centre how they do, after so few days, to change their minds so loudly when they vote against this amendment, which, in fact, says only one thing, namely that those who have been expelled with escort, who return to Italy and commit another crime cannot be favored by a new expulsion, but must, as it should be, atone for their punishment in prison. I do not understand, in other words, how the New Right Centre makes, a few days later, to be so rigid in relation to the provision on alternative measures to prison and now - today - on this decree-law instead to be so lax. In fact, let one thing be clear: thanks also to your vote, the foreigners expelled with accompaniment who will return to Italy and who will commit a crime for which they will be sentenced to two years of imprisonment, will obviously be expelled and will not atone for any penalty.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u271	Barani (GAL). Mr President, Mr Undersecretary, ladies and gentlemen, today we are faced with yet another measure on the rights of prisoners and against the overcrowding of the prison population. These are serious problems, on several occasions and from several parts denounced, also from the undersigned and also from the Head of State, the president Napolitano, in the message to the Chambers. The Head of State would have told us he had more courage, he would have asked us for indult and amnesty, but I realize that the majority of this House is a catto-communist majority, but not communist to Togliatti, that the amnesty had the courage to do it in 1946, but those that I consider to be worse. While this does not show the sensitivity of Parliament and Government on such issues, it is a bit better than nothing. It can only be welcomed. Among other things, the continuous intervention on the subject makes clear the real enormousness of the phenomenon of overcrowding, which is a real torture. We're torturing prisoners. A hateful phenomenon that violates the most basic principles of dignity of the human person, as pointed out not by myself, but by Europe, ready to sanction us, to penalise the Italian citizens economically because we do not have the courage to make structural decisions such as indult and amnesty. If we do not act quickly, our citizens will have to pay Europe the sanctions that are of a certain economic value per capita, and on this there is a deadline: May of this year. For the umpteenth time in this House, therefore, I remember, as I have already done before, the message with which the President of the Republic invited us to a thorough reflection. I was pleased that President Palma, reporting the government's data, said that at most 3,500 prisoners were released because an amendment was approved that increased the roof from 4 to 5 years in order to serve custody, because I remember that it was the amendment signed by the undersigned, presented by the Great Autonomous and Freedom Group, which is the only one that managed structurally to lead to this reduction of the prison population, even with the favorable opinion of the Government, and I acknowledge the undersecretary Berretta of the courage he had, which was praised in the message to the House also by the President of the Republic. In view of the enormous impact on the prison population of the measures envisaged in the field of drugs, without going into the substance of a suitable distinction between heavy and light drugs (he who speaks to you has signed the referendums of the radicals), it seems logical to me the choice to introduce an autonomous case of crime for what is currently an attenuating to the crime of possession and dealing of drugs and that therefore saw the penalties already considerably reduced. A few days after a statement by the Council on this matter, I think it is also appropriate for us to consider how about 40% of the prison population between defendants and convicted is composed of prisoners for violation of the drug law. As a doctor, I cannot help but make the necessary distinctions between the social danger and the health of a dose of badly cut heroin and a dose of cannabis, just to give an example. The same methods and equipment necessary for the production and subsequent sale of the substances mentioned above are very different and therefore need to be treated differently, but it will be a task for Parliament in the near future. Finally, the decree-law provides for the custody of social services in order to obtain therapeutic recovery for drug addicts, a prescription that seems to me to be fully part of the constitutional provisions for which penalties must tend to re-education. I remind everyone of Article 27 (3) and Article 13 (4) of our Constitution, which many often forget. The same change in the time limits as regards the probationary custody of social services from penalties not exceeding three years to penalties not exceeding four years seems to me and seems to us, as a Group of Great Autonomies and Freedoms, to fit well into the framework already outlined with the other measures recently approved in this field. You see, ladies and gentlemen, institutions such as testing or relying on social services are all perfectly in line with both our internal requirements and European directives, especially with regard to the objective of re-education of the condemned and the idea of inflicting a penalty which, in any case, never annihilates the individual. There are people in our penitentiaries who are literally rotting in jail, and I do not believe that a civil state and a society can allow such a state of affairs to continue, especially in relation to certain categories of crime, which are then the ones on which we have intervened and are intervening. There should also be an in-depth reflection on the much-deserved certainty of punishment: of what certainty do you want to exquisite in the presence of clearly excessive penalties or begin to serve already before a sentence that is never known if it will ever arrive? This is the case, for example, with preventive imprisonment. I remember that 40 percent of the prisoners are awaiting trial with preventive imprisonment, and of this 40 percent 50 percent are then judged innocent. I'm talking about 12,000 people: what a shame! Firstly, the penalty must be imposed in a proportionate manner to the crime committed. Secondly, if the penitentiaries are unable to accommodate new prison population, why are they full of foreigners, drug addicts, inmates awaiting trial or guilty of minor crimes, how can we ensure that those who have committed a serious crime are subjected to the intended penalty? The right, ladies and gentlemen, is nothing more than the regulation of civil life, and precisely from the concept of civilization, social and legal, we must start when we legislate, that is when we intervene on the law. The Grandi Autonomie e Libertà Group is convinced of civilization as to the appropriateness of this provision and although the tight times do not leave room for improvements in the text that we considered appropriate - because because of the now dominant monocameralism, it is the Chamber that decides and we ratify - my Group will vote in favour because we think that a little 'better than nothing and that anyway the excellent is enemy of the good. Finally, I think I can say that if we had the courage to put in place the civil responsibility of the judges, the prison overcrowding would be no longer there, because they would be careful and think twice before placing custody for those who are then found innocent. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u272	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u273	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u274	ALBERTINI (PI). Mr President, why empty prisons? Let's start with arithmetic data: as of November 30, 2013, we had 64,047 prisoners against a regulatory capacity of just under 48,000; on September 30, there were over 24,000 prisoners in custody. However, there are not only these elements. The European Court of Human Rights, in its well-known judgment in Torreggiani in January 2013, also found that our country had injured Article 3 of the European Convention on Human Rights, which states that no one can be subjected to torture or inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment. The overcrowding of prisons is therefore not only a cogency for the numbers I have mentioned, but also a humanitarian emergency attested by a highly qualified source such as the European Court. The Italian State must, within one year of the entry into force of the final judgment, give appropriate provisions for this infringement to be eliminated or, at least, largely reduced. This judgment also provided the necessary opportunities to compensate those who had been subjected to these inhuman treatments concerning the situation of prison overcrowding in which our prison system is located. It has been found in this provision of the Court of Justice that even in terms of compensation and reparation, our country complies with the addresses of the European Court of Justice and the international conventions it has signed. Italy must comply by May 28 of this year, as has been mentioned in the interventions and perhaps also because it is the causality of a decree-law. Not only has the European Court imposed on us this series of interventions, but it was the Constitutional Court itself which intended to remedy the overcrowding of prisons by its recent ruling. The Government, during the current legislature, has already intervened on this issue (I remember Decree-Law No. 78 of 2013) and Parliament has addressed the issue in the context of the revision of precautionary measures, non-convict prison sentences and testing. The decree-law under consideration completes, as it were, this trilogy with changes to the code of criminal procedure and to the penitentiary order (in particular, the single text for the treatment of crimes related to the use of drugs and immigration). It then establishes - as the Court of Justice requires us, but also our legal civilization - the National Guarantor of Prisoners, in order to promote the possibility of expressing rights even in conditions of detention. Extension of the time limits for the adoption of the regulations relating to tax credits and the contributory reductions of the social cooperatives taking on prisoners. More guarantees are therefore offered for both administrative and judicial protection before the judge for the supervision of prisoners. The National Guarantor of Prisoners must ensure his independence - and this possibility is clearly indicated in the legal provisions - and he has the power to supervise the conditions of prison institutions; he has the power to carry out visits and to intervene, as a supervisory body, on the proper administration of justice even in the prison through recommendations and must give account to the Chambers of his intervention. A simplified procedure has also been developed in this decree-law for the treatment of matters falling within the competence of the supervisory judiciary, precisely to give greater protection to the rights of prisoners. There is then finally a disposition that had for a long time put mold, so to speak, using a current expression: it is a strange case, that of our country, where the electronic bracelet has been denied by our system - while in other countries it is used with great utility and cost savings - and has never worked by us, despite the expensive contracts that predicted it. Well, the measure in question gives the court the right to prescribe the electronic bracelet as a general rule and not as an exception. Then we have the transformation into an autonomous crime, with regard to the connection with the sale of small quantities of drugs: this too can be such an argument that a large number of prisoners, who can find outside the prison walls possibility of redemption because of their difficult situation, can be removed from the prison. The inapplicability of compulsory arrest in the event of a flagrant event has also been introduced. I would like to begin by concluding quickly, with a view to keeping the House's attention within the limits of the time limit, given the time, to say that the measure under consideration is necessary and could not fail to be adopted, not only for the sake of cogency, but also for a fact of legal civilization. We have not all agreed on certain rules or amendments, not even we who support the majority, and we are asking ourselves about some cases where the Government has also expressed an opinion against reasons which seemed to us to be of complete and absolute rationality. A convicted for an aggravated robbery, since expulsion is not mandatory, can safely remain in our territory after being expelled: we do not like that this can be passed. As for some particular crimes and also for all of the crimes, the passage from 45 to 75 days of penalty discount, for sentences of just under two years, is almost equivalent to the cancellation of half of the sentence. These are also arguments that are a little forced - so to speak - to achieve the purpose of emptying prisons, but not entirely rational. I conclude by saying that our Group will, however, vote in favour of the measure being voted on, but without giving up its criticism - where it felt it was - and above all hopes that this series of interventions, which are organic as a whole, but not complete and not structural in their connection, will find a better settlement and a more appropriate rationalization in the next legislative term. [[Applause from Senator Romano.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u284	D'ASCOLA (NCD). Mr President, in the world from which I come, if you repeat the things spoken by others or take the floor or hold your shoulders as a sign of pity. I will not repeat, therefore, the things said, on which I agree, for the part of the declarations in favour of the conversion into law of the decree-law that we are dealing. I would just like to say something critical of the objections raised against the decree in various ways. It has been said that the decree would irreversibly affect a fundamental principle of the State, that of the certainty of the penalty. However, it should be noted that the Italian State has for years renounced this principle: crime and punishment no longer constitute an absolutely indissoluble link. The State uses the offence to monetize the non-execution of convictions through condons; the State uses the non-application of the sentence as a function of stimulating collaboration with the judicial authority. In short, the certainty of the sentence belongs to a criminal law of the past, certainly not to a criminal law of the present. It would, of course, be appropriate to take note of this when we remember fundamental principles that no longer find a consideration in the current requirements of a criminal law that has now, at least for forty years, been transformed in this direction. Among other things, we must not forget an extremely important circumstance, which is a demonstration of a kind of circuit, or rather a short circuit in which this difficult and complex discipline pours: the penalties that our penal system provides, but above all those that daily legislative assemblies introduce, are exorbitant only that the European panorama is taken into account. We address ourselves to the European Union for many different reasons; we neglect to consider that our penalties are at least a third higher than those which are applied within states to which we should, on the contrary, look, like France, Germany and the United Kingdom. The short mental circuit is just as follows: we have stratospheric penalties, we propose further ones because we dismiss legislative texts that provide for penalties absolutely higher than the average European ones, then inevitably we have to reduce them. Perhaps the serious point of the matter should be to understand, in the meantime, that the affirmation of criminal liability should be guaranteed by a system that, compared to the current one, is more able to select the certain responsibility of the sentenced person than the cases of uncertain liability which should hesitate in declaring judgments of the defendant's non-liability. Therefore, the guarantee should certainly refer to the phases of the investigation, to penalties actually commensurate with the gravity of the event and taking into account the principle of reeducation. Of course, at that point, a system of automatic reduction of convictions would make no sense. But - I repeat - madness consists precisely in this: we have exorbitant penalties; we are proud to introduce even more exorbitant ones only that we control the legislative texts that this Parliament dismisses; then it is inevitable that we realize a problem, a real problem, namely that prison is a social problem. We cannot forget this obvious statement. Our criminal law is excessive quantitatively, the penalties are too high. The error lies in the intervention in the final phase, in the phase of execution, but it is the only solution that can be pursued in a situation in which, for the stage of ascertaining responsibility, but even earlier for the phase dedicated to the prediction of the conducts considered worthy of punishment and the quantification of the related sanctions, we follow - for a verse - a kind of policy of panpenalism, for which everything is criminal law, and - on the side of the commeasurement of the sentences - the maximum edittal limits are chosen. Returning to a subject that has occupied this House a great deal, if prison is an emergency, if prison is a social problem comparable to the health problem, in this case, in fact - I believe - there were all the reasons for urgency, the need to provide. In any case, compared to an introductory decree of improvement measures for prisoners, the non-conversion into law would have had a meaning entirely equivalent to the introduction of a punitive Peer Intervention for those who had already enjoyed such measures, given the immediate effectiveness related to decree-laws. A pejorative intervention certainly could not be allowed. In short, the need to download on the stage of execution that necessary relationship of reasonableness between fact and sanction and the difficulty of denying favorable effects that had already been introduced through the use of a decree-law, made necessary by the emergency prison - and therefore by an emergency that inevitably indicated in itself the reasons for necessity and urgency - justify the favorable vote that the Group to which I honour to belong declares to release regarding the decree that we are dealing with. [[Applause from the NCD Group.]]

This isn't right. All this the Italians will understand. Our vote will definitely be against and we hope that the damage to this country will be as limited as possible. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u293	GINETTI (PD). Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, in this legislative year we have repeatedly stressed how the issue of justice was central in order to get our country out of the situation of special supervision by a European Union which in its documents has classified us in the last positions for effectiveness and efficiency; a central issue for a modern and conscious country is that the functioning of justice is first and foremost a guarantee of fundamental human rights, but it is also a factor of economic development, because the ability to attract capital, especially in a period of crisis, is a flywheel of recovery. What we would have liked to address the serious problem of the overcrowding of prisons, which of justice represents the stage of criminal execution (after the sentence Torreggiani that condemned us for inhumane and degrading treatment and after the reference to the political responsibility of the President of the Republic in his message to the Chambers), was an organic measure, system and that succeeded in affecting, innovative the system of penalties, in a coordinated way, with changes to the code of criminal procedure and the penitentiary order. Instead, we intervened with separate acts and at different times, while affecting key issues such as decriminalization, the introduction of home detention as the main penalty, together with the institute of suspension of the trial and testing. Graduality of the criminal penalty, certainty and effectiveness of the penalty and fulfillment of the function that the Constitution itself assigns to criminal execution, that is, reeducation and social reintegration: these are the public values and interests that we have been called to contend in this first year. The data provided by the Ministry of Justice confirm, however, that in the last twelve years the total population held has ranged from 56,000 to 61,000 today. This means that, in fact, we should have considered for some time already exceeded the number of 47,000 places currently in prisons and that we should have planned the necessary enlargements already for years, the approximately 12,000 new places that the same 2010 prison plan would have had to implement. The same data confirm that 40% of prisoners are detained for a protective custody measure, pending final judgment (symptom of the need to reconsider the parameters for assessing the adequacy and proportionality of the precautionary measure), as is a certain fact that 38% of prisoners are serving a penalty for violation of the single drug law. The decision of the Constitutional Court that declared the Giovanardi-Fini law illegitimate will certainly have repercussions on a number of prisoners who are estimated to be around a few thousand (we will have to wait for the motivation). The Government, with Decree-Law No 146 at our examination, chooses to adopt urgent and extraordinary measures for the controlled reduction of the prison population through the provision, as a general rule, of the use of the electronic bracelet for house arrests and for house detention; it simplifies the investigation procedures of the judiciary in the granting of benefits; it guarantees greater certainty regarding the right to claim prisoners; it repeals the automatism of the prohibition to dispose of more than twice the measure of custody in evidence for therapeutic purposes for drug addicts, repealing which, combined with the reduction of penalty for crimes of detention and small dealing, will produce, in concrete terms, an important deflective effect. The introduction of the case of early release as an autonomous benefit, with a penalty discount of 75 days per semester, and an extraordinary measure because it applies for a duration limited to two years (even if retroactive to 2010), has actually raised doubts as to its applicability to all types of crime. These concerns have been resolved by the establishment of a double track (so we can call it), because it excluded the most dangerous crimes referred to in Article 4-a of the prison order; as highlighted by the Parent Company in Commission Lumia, it already contains the inclusion of the crimes committed in accordance with the procedures laid down in Article 416-a. However, it will certainly be the task of the legislator, once the new Article 416-b has been finally adopted in the Chamber of Deputies, to incorporate the new case into the provisions of Article 4-a of the penitentiary order. The figure of the National Guarantor goes in the direction of strengthening the guarantees and the protection of the prisoners, but in fact there is some doubt about the fact that it has been incardinated in the Ministry of Justice, although having provided all the guarantees of impartiality and autonomy of its functions. Finally, given the strong incidence of the presence of foreigners in our 206 institutions, the extension of the scope of the expulsion will have the effect of reducing the population held, provided that the necessary and preventive agreements with the States of origin for penalties over two years, extension from the scope that will have to provide for a coordination between the two cases of expulsion as an alternative measure and as a substitute sanction, but with a deflective effect also in this extremely important case. Ladies and gentlemen, in May our country will be called upon to demonstrate that it has taken appropriate measures to put an end to the overcrowding and inhumane conditions of detention in our prisons. For this reason, the Democratic Party Group will vote in favour of the conversion of Decree-Law No. 146, which shows that it has taken a positive path to innovate the system of criminal execution useful to avoid the phenomenon of revolving doors in the entrances to the prison and to decrease the total number of prisoners. There is still a lack of intervention that brings back the penitentiary treatment, with the training and work activities, inside and outside prisons, and the socializing detention regimes, at the centre of the system of rehabilitation of the sentence. Prioritary, therefore, is the realization of regional differentiated circuits by type of treatment and of prisoners. I turn to the Government: this presupposes not only rethinking the closure of some minor structures intended for attenuated custody, but also rethinking the process of reorganization in place of the same regional authorities, with garrisons of contact with the territories, in a system that sees in the external execution the fulcrum of the criminal execution. I am therefore beginning to conclude: we must also speed up and not postpone the operation of the fund that supports the recruitment of prisoners with tax reductions, which has been stationary for too long. Although we have taken the right path, it still seems that it is not the penitentiary system that is in emergency by overcrowding, but that in emergency it is our difficulty to plan first, and then to implement, in a coherent and linear way, a plan of adaptation and civilization of the planet prisons. This is the political witness that, with our vote today, we hand over to the next government of the country. [[Applause from the PD Group and Senator Buemi. Congratulations.]]
