ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u306	Mr President, representatives of the Government, colleagues, the draft law under consideration provides for the conversion into law, with amendments, of Decree-Law No 145 of 23 December 2013, with urgent measures to start the Destination Italia plan, for the containment of electricity and gas tariffs, for the internationalization, development and digitalization of companies, as well as measures for the realization of public works and Expo 2015. This measure, which even today arrives in the Chamber in a situation that has partially changed (all of us know the events of our Government), has great importance and importance, because it contains, as I have just said, measures that, although not decisive regarding the need to restart the economy of our country severely tested by the crisis, certainly have importance and possibility to intervene in a concrete way on areas decisive for development and growth. It is therefore important - and I stress this at the beginning of my brief speech - that the Senate should consider it today, albeit with a very short time (I say this immediately) compared to what we would have liked to have had, because the House of Deputies, with a deprecable practice of ahinoi, once again, held this measure for analysis and discussion for longer than it would have been right. I repeat that this is an important measure, and even in this critical political situation, we have appreciated that the majority, and I say this without rhetoric, have contributed a substantial part of the opposition to its much facilitated process in the Chamber of Deputies and, here in the Senate, in the Commission. I would like to thank my colleagues in the Commissions meeting 6a and 10a for having dealt with this matter, even during these days of consultations and work, in a timely manner. I would also like to thank my colleagues from the 5 Star Movement who, at their first sitting, gave us a detailed explanation of all their amendments, but who have very responsibly ensured that the Commissions together proceed in their work and then come to present the report today in a convincing manner, with the assessments that the majority have expressed. I shall now proceed to the illustration for brief items. It is a measure that has been talked about a great deal, but it is good to recall its crucial elements. My colleague, Mrs Fabbri, will complete the analysis in a timely manner, while I will focus mainly on the analysis of the articles which mainly concern the Finance and Treasury Commission, of which I am a member. The draft law comes at second reading and, with regard to the parts of greater interest for the 6th Commission, it is first mentioned paragraph 1 of Article 2, which reform the discipline of incentives for self-entrepreneurship (referred to in Title I of Legislative Decree No. 185 of 21 April 2000), with measures aimed mainly at supporting the creation and development, through better conditions of access to credit, of small enterprises owned mainly by young people and women. Individuals who can access the benefits must meet a number of requirements. The first is that of the innovation of the enterprise: they must be enterprises constituted for no more than 12 months on the date of submission of the application for facilitation. Here there was an extension by the Chamber of Deputies of the decree-law presented by the Government. Initiatives involving investments of up to EUR 1 500 000 relating to the production of goods in the sectors of industry, crafts and processing of agricultural products or to the provision of services to businesses, as well as initiatives relating to further sectors identified in the implementing decree, are eligible for financing. The House of Deputies has, on the one hand, extended the scope of the incentives for self-entrepreneurship (even trade and tourism initiatives have been added in that forum) and, on the other hand, has removed the clause of financial invariance that was in the text novelized by the decree-law in the original version. Article 24 of Legislative Decree No 185 of 21 April 2000 already provided that the Minister for the Treasury, the Budget and Economic Planning, in agreement with the Minister for Labour and Social Security (and, with regard to the relevant provisions, including the Minister for Agricultural and Forestry Policies), would lay down, by means of one or more regulations, criteria and arrangements for granting facilities. The House of Deputies has introduced paragraph 1-a in order to allocate a share of EUR 20 million to women's businesses, but I believe that Mrs Fabbri will be able much better than me to illustrate these innovative elements. Article 3 establishes a tax credit for companies investing in research and development activities up to a total limit of €600 million for the three-year period 2014-2016. The measure, in summary, provides a tax credit of 50% of the incremental costs incurred by companies compared to the previous year, with a maximum concession of €2.5 million per enterprise and a minimum expenditure of € 50,000 in research and development to be able to access the facility. You all understand that these are modes introduced in a logic of support also to the small and very small enterprise. This is a fact that seems to me to be extremely important, given that these are the companies that are experiencing the most difficulties in recent years and also difficulties in innovation. The use of the facility takes place through a computer platform: these are also innovations aimed at simplification (which, on several occasions, we all, I believe, emphasized in the different Commissions in the past months). Article 4 to paragraph 1 rewrites the discipline for the industrial conversion of the national polluted sites of prominent public interest and paragraph 2 provides for the granting of a tax credit to the subscribed companies of the program agreements, referred to in Article 252- bis of Legislative Decree no. 152 of 2006, against the acquisition of new capital goods from the tax period following that in progress on the date of entry into force of this decree and until the end of the tax period of 31 December 2015, under the conditions that the norm defines. The same paragraph obliges the allocation of the tax credit to the fulfilment of very precise and precise conditions; in particular, it is required that the enterprises be newly formed. Paragraphs 3 to 10 lay down detailed rules for the allocation of this tax credit. Article 5 (2) allows for a wider articulation of the opening hours of border customs offices, with reference to all goods moving under procedures other than transit. Here again, there are changes introduced to the Chamber of Deputies to provide that administrative procedures are carried out at the same time as the presentation of goods for the purpose of completing customs formalities and are concluded within precise deadlines for which non-compliance is provided for a sanction. These - I stress - are all elements strongly demanded by the same companies operating in these sectors and aimed at certainty and cutting red tape. Paragraphs 4 to 14 of Article 6 provide for a deduction from gross tax in the percentage of 65% of the amounts remaining to be borne by the taxpayer up to a maximum value of EUR 20,000 for documented expenditure incurred by small and medium-sized enterprises, or consortia thereof, relating to fixed and mobile network operations aimed at ensuring a connectivity speed with capacity equal to or greater than 30 megabits per second. The House then stated that the provision of tax credit for the connectivity of small and medium-sized enterprises can also take place in the context of the planning of national interventions financed by the Development and Cohesion Fund and the Rotation Fund for the implementation of Community policies. Paragraph 11 refers to a decree of the Minister of Economic Development the methods of taking advantage of the credit. The House has deleted the forecast that the ministerial decree to define the modalities of the provision of the tax credit was subject to the approval of the national operational programme by the European Commission. Paragraph 12 provides for the non-cumulability of the deduction with the facility provided by paragraph 1. According to paragraph 13, the tax credit must be indicated in the tax return relating to the tax period during which the benefit has matured; it does not contribute to the formation of income, it does not contribute to the formation of the tax base for the purposes of IRAP, it does not detect for the purpose of determining the relationship between revenues and income, on the one hand, and the total amount of all revenue and income on the other hand; it is only usable in the context of the compensation between debts and tax credits. Paragraph 14 provides that the resources to be used for the provision of the tax credit are paid at the entry of the State budget to be reallocated to a special programme of expenditure of the Ministry of Economy and Finance. The provisions of Article 7 (this, moreover, is a particularly important and important article which, in some respects, introduces a different way in the relationship between companies and tax) intend to extend access to the preventive agreements between the taxpayer and the Agency of the revenue on taxation (the so-called ruling) of international standard, allowing the taxpayer to activate the ruling procedure also with reference to the request for assessment, in advance, of the existence or not of the requirements that constitute a permanent establishment located in the territory of the State, having regard to the criteria provided for by the current rules. Article 9, also amended during the examination in the Chamber, provides the possibility to activate a tax credit for the purchase of books, even in digital format. It is little compared to what was demanded in support of the families, that they are another subject that today needs a help for the restart of the Country, but it is a specific signal in the context of the widening of the cultural challenge and the support to the incomes in a particularly difficult situation. I am not detailing it here, but Article 9 sets out the ways and means of accessing this tax credit, which will also be done in rather simplified forms and with a maximum of EUR 50 million in funding for the facility, which is part of a dedicated national operational programme for the 2014-2020 programming of the Community Structural Funds. Article 12, which was also amended by the House, contains a set of measures designed, as a whole, to bring liquidity into the business system and to feed the financing channels of small and medium-sized enterprises. This objective is first pursued through a reform of the securitisation framework, as set out in Law No 130 of 1999. Among the main amendments, the provisions that extend the securitisation framework also to transactions involving bonds and similar securities signed by so-called special purpose vehicles, those that apply the capital segregation rule also to the possible bankruptcy of the entity in charge of collecting the transferred claims and cash and payment services and of the bank on which the securitisation company maintains its deposits. Simplifications are also made to the rules governing the securitisation of corporate loans and the transfer of loans to public administration. In order to encourage the investment of pension funds and insurance companies in bond securities, it is possible to calculate among the assets admitted to cover the technical provisions of insurance undertakings the securities issued in the framework of securitisation transactions made through the subscription and purchase of similar bonds and securities, even if not intended to be traded on a regulated market or in multilateral trading systems and even without credit ratings. The provisions referred to in paragraph 2 affect the discipline of financial instruments (the so-called minibonds and bonds) contained in Article 32 of Decree-Law No 83 of 2012, in particular by encouraging the investment in such financial instruments by insurance companies and pension funds. As you will understand, this is an important tool to ensure access to credit for those companies that are increasingly struggling to find it, even with the support of the traditional banking system. Paragraph 3 simplifies, in the case of transfers of credits made pursuant to the Factoring Act, the formalities for obtaining the certain date of payment and therefore for the opponibility of the transfer to third parties, making less onerous bureaucracy for the incumbent companies. Paragraphs 4 and 5 reform the tax regime applicable to medium- and long-term financing, which are currently subject to indirect tax substitution. In particular, one of the main innovations is the extension of the substitute tax to structured financing operations. Paragraph 6 extends the special privilege on movable property intended for the operation of the company, provided to guarantee the financing of the banks to the enterprises, also to guarantee the issuance of bonds and similar securities. Paragraph 6-a allows the Guarantee Fund for small and medium-sized enterprises to provide guarantees in favour of savings management companies. Paragraph 7 carries the financial coverage of the article under consideration, while paragraph 7-a refers to a subsequent ministerial decree to identify the modalities to compensate, in the year 2014, the debitorial records in favour of companies holding non-prescribed claims, certain, liquid and due, accrued against the public administration, if the amount entered in the role is lower or equal to the credit boasted. Turning finally to Article 13, again as far as the Finance and Treasury Commission is concerned, paragraphs 16 to 18 exempt passengers from the additional municipal tax on the boarding rights of passengers in airports, as well as from the additional commissioner for Rome capital, passengers in transit in national airports if they come from domestic ports of call. As you can see, so many different elements with regard to their nature, but absolutely coherent - I repeat, absolutely coherent - with regard to the objective they pursue: to go together to allocate resources, to limit elements of bureaucracy that have manifested themselves as impediments and to allow, through new instruments, access to credit and resources in particular to small and medium-sized enterprises. I therefore end by stressing that the decree-law in question, which I hope can be converted into law today, which is also presented to us in a form that for lack of time forces us to the compactness and defense of the text that came from the House, is a good text and is extremely useful - we believe - to allow the start of a new phase. To some extent - and I say this also in the face of misunderstandings - it is a text that can strongly mark political continuity. In some ways, it is a measure that represents the best of the Government that is coming to the end of its work and we hope it can represent to some extent beginning to continue to improve and strengthen the attempts, here included, to give concrete signals to the citizens of recovery and recovery of the Italian economy. [[Applause from the PD Group.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u307	I call the rapporteur, Senator Fabbri.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u308	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, we are discussing this decree-law as we move towards a change in the government structure, a sudden and traumatic change in some respects. I do not enter into the political debate, but one thing I want to emphasize: what is happening, the dynamic that is developing and that I hope will soon lead to the birth of a new Executive, finds part of its reasons in the economic and social situation, dramatic, that still lives our country. From the ISTAT report "We Italy 2014", presented a few days ago and relating to the economic situation, the labour market and the business system, emerges a picture still gloomy: one in four families is in conditions of deprivation, people of working age who actually have a job are six out of ten, GDP per capita, with purchasing power parity, is below the average of the European Union. And to this we must add a level of taxation that reaches the levels of Sweden, noting, however, that the same services are not offered in return. It would be enough for these data to certify the need for a positive shock for the country. I believe that, apart from political controversy and legitimate confrontation, it would be irresponsible not to grasp the urgency and need of a strong and authoritative Government that helps Italy to rise again. We will see times and ways of forming the government, but the deadlines and priorities indicated by the prime minister in charge (institutions, work, public administration and tax) seem to me to be fundamental tracks on which to make the train of reforms walk. Of course, then there are also early signs timidly encouraging. It would suffice those on GDP in the fourth quarter of 2013 to tell us that something is moving, or the recent photograph taken by the information system "Excelsior" realized by Unioncamere and the Ministry of Labour, based on the forecasts of employment in industry and services for the first quarter 2014, which shows how the economic conditions of our country are slowly but constantly improving. The crisis must pass and the country must restart, and with it employment levels. The expectations of a possible increase in exports push companies with more than 50 employees operating in different sectors of manufacturing made in Italy to hire, such as mechanics companies that provide 3,000 additional jobs or those of innovative services and support to business activities. Let me give you a gender perspective: thanks to the Guarantee Fund for small and medium-sized enterprises dedicated to women's businesses, entrepreneurs will be able to directly access finance in a simplified way and present themselves to banks or trusts with the reservation of the State guarantee. There are also important signs of the internationalisation of our companies. According to Unioncamere, there are over 70,000 Italian companies whose products and business strategies would be ready to cross national borders with positive implications for economic recovery that do not escape anyone. We therefore call for the new Government to support these first signs of recovery and to provide the country with a revival that will ensure well-being and stability for its citizens for the years to come. A revival that, of course, cannot be without a profound re-thinking of the credit system for businesses and individuals, because we cannot hide the fact that the banking crunch on access to credit certainly does not help the recovery of the economy, after a period of crisis like what we have gone through and are going through. But what is needed today is a push and acceleration that accompanies important measures such as what we are discussing: we are not yet out of the swamp and if we want these first reversals of trend to become more consistent, we must run. I would like to refer to some of the most significant measures without retracing those already introduced and explained by my colleague Senator Olivero. In particular, Article 1 shows some measures aimed at reducing electricity prices; the hourly prices for customers under the highest protection regime are revised; the minimum guaranteed prices for the withdrawal scheme dedicated to the hourly zonal price are standardized. Very important and awaited measures by the Italian population, citizens and businesses. Other provisions concern geothermal energy, Sulcis' coal-fired power station, with a specific incentive for the construction of a carbon dioxide storage power station, and the obligation to release biofuels that is frozen for 2014 to avoid the increase of half a percentage point in fuel prices. Article 2, to which I am linked and which I consider very important, reform the regulation of incentives for self-entrepreneurship, with measures to support the creation and development, through better conditions of access to credit, of small enterprises owned and conducted mainly by young people and women. The incentives are now applicable throughout the national territory; in fact, the provision restricting its application to the less-favoured areas of the country is deleted. As regards the benefits, they consist of granting soft loans for investments at zero rates for a maximum duration of 8 years and for an amount not exceeding 75% of the expenditure deemed eligible under Community legislation. New companies and, with the changes made to the Chamber, companies constituted no more than 12 months on the date of submission of the application can also access the benefits, elevating this requirement which, in the original wording of the text, provided for 6 months. It must be micro and small enterprises, which are - I remember - the economic backbone of our country. The intention is to give more and more opportunities for those who want to develop entrepreneurial initiatives, so much so that the House has extended the measure also to initiatives in the field of trade and tourism, exceeding also the clause of financial invariance previously previewed. Let me say this: we will have to work very carefully in the future and with regard to the tourism sector, as they fly for a new economic development, in order not only to recognise its value, but with the aim of increasing its potential (I remind my colleagues that tourism represents more than 10% of current GDP). Article 3 of the measure establishes a tax credit - as Senator Olivero recalled - for companies investing in research and development activities. In summary, the measure provides for a tax credit of 50% of the incremental costs incurred by companies compared to the previous year. On the other hand, Article 4 contains measures concerning the realization of the reclamations of sites of national interest, favouring programme agreements between the Ministry of Economic Development and the regions concerned for the recovery of contaminated areas and industrial conversion. Article 5 provides for measures to support the internationalisation of enterprises, increasing by 22.5 million for 2014 the resources of the Fund for the promotion of trade and internationalisation, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises. The opening hours of border customs offices are extended, in some cases 24 hours a day. Agricultural and fishery enterprises are also included in the consortia for internationalization; the framework of the powers of the Chambers of Commerce is also changed, giving them, in compliance with the powers conferred by law on other public administrations, the power to issue certificates for the free sale and marketing of products on the Italian or Community territory. Article 6 introduces a voucher of no more than EUR 10,000 to facilitate the digitisation and connectivity of small and medium-sized enterprises. A rather significant rule is introduced in Article 9 to encourage the dissemination of reading among children. Finally, Article 12 sets out a series of measures aimed, as a whole, at providing liquidity to the business system and, in particular, at feeding the financing channels of small and medium-sized enterprises. The decree extends the principle of the capital autonomy of the vehicle companies also to the case of securitisation of claims arising from the issuance of financial bills, thus relaunching an instrument capable of allowing SMEs alternative forms of financing to the banking system itself. It is also foreseen - as we have mentioned - the possibility to issue minibonds. In particular, the issuing of a decree of the Ministry of Economy and Finance, in agreement with the Ministry of Economic Development, aimed at defining the modalities for the compensation for the year 2014 of the debitorial records of the companies holding non-prescribed credits, certain, liquid and payable, accrued against the public administration, if the amount entered in the role is lower or equal to the credit boasted. Article 13 also contains a series of important measures for the realization of the works for Expo, an extraordinary and fundamental event for our country. Finally there are planned interventions for the Campania Region, the realization of line 1 of the metro of Naples and the railway service. Finally, Article 14 lays down measures to combat undeclared and irregular work. In conclusion, Mr President, we are faced with a measure which clearly sets itself the objective of supporting the Italian production system at a time of great difficulty, but at a time when the economy tries to restart. Through simplifications, cost reduction and incentives, the decree wants to make it easier to "do business" through tools that can help to engage the economic recovery. And try to do this by working on a structural strengthening of the production system. We are not faced with an omnibus decree, but with a measure that seeks, through targeted interventions, to improve the competitiveness of Italian companies. Maybe it's not much. Certainly it is little, it is still too little; but we have the duty to know and enhance our economic fabric, made of artisans and small entrepreneurs, made of those who choose "intrapresa" as a source of development not only economic, but also social. For this reason we believe that it is an important decree, therefore, whose same name, Destinazione Italia, tells us that the will is to assist the economic subjects, creating on the one hand the structural conditions for our companies to return to invest and to grow and on the other the environmental ones to try to return the desire to foreign subjects to engage also in our country. It's been a long time, it's true. But I remember that yesterday morning in Piazza del Popolo in Rome there was an event of Rete imprese Italia, which is estimated to have participated 50-60,000 entrepreneurs. It is with that sensitivity and in order to respond to those needs that I believe is the right choice to value this decree, albeit with all its limitations, and to approve it in a useful time. [[Applause from the PD, SCPI and PI groups.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u312	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, living abroad I think I am in a privileged position to observe the effects of the interventions of Italian institutions aimed at promoting our country and the difficulties encountered by foreign investors in Italy. After all, this is the purpose of the decree-law Destinazione Italia. In these months I have had the opportunity to meet many brave entrepreneurs in Italy, who have succeeded in achieving without any help or assistance from the State. I asked what was their biggest problem, particularly with regard to the internationalisation of their activities, and the most common answer I heard from them is that the greatest difficulty was and continues to be the continuing battle against bureaucracy, which makes it difficult to start and manage their activities and very often distracts them from the entrepreneurial role to which they should devote themselves instead to 110%. Honestly, that doesn't surprise me. Italy is the eighth industrial power in the world and is the fourth in Europe; it is part of the G8 and the G20. Unfortunately, when it comes to judging the ease of doing business, the numbers change: we are no longer in the club of eight or twenty. According to the World Bank's "Doing Business" report, our country is 65th in 189 economies; in Europe countries such as Bulgaria or Poland exceed us. Even in the tax sector, in trying to collect taxpayers' taxes, our performance is not among the best: we are 138th in 189. But there is another very important criterion: that of enforcing commercial contracts; well, Italy is in 103rd place out of 189. In other words, the message we send to the world is that there is no legal certainty in Italy. Mr President, the decree we are examining today is in the right direction, but it is not enough, as the rapporteur said before. To facilitate internationalisation processes, to attract foreign investment and capable human resources we need to do more. First of all, we must start a process of simplifying the legal and administrative apparatus. Foreign companies investing in Italy often find themselves implated in slow, complicated and often discouraging procedures. Today Italy, as a nation, is a resource of itself, with its cultural and territorial heritage. I am a witness to how many companies would like to invest in our territory but many of them, unfortunately, because of administrative and bureaucratic complications, have renounced it. The new Government must address the issue of simplification and harmonisation of procedures, including from Region to Region, as a priority. I welcome the commitment of the President-in-Office of the Council and hope that this will lead to a change of mind: the public administration must be at the service of the citizen. This is the only precondition for simplification. The second field of attention and intervention is the use of human and financial resources to promote our country around the world. In the report accompanying the decree Destinazione Italia, I read: "For an economic diplomacy aimed at attracting foreign investments." But who are the economic diplomats of Italy? Many of our entrepreneurs, many of our companies promote the Italian system independently, with brilliant ideas that often share culture and product, using the resources of Italians abroad on the territory. And when I speak of resources, I am also referring to those who have reached the highest levels of representation in their country of adoption. This morning I sent to all Members a message of the recent experience of a group of Italian wine-growers in Australia. I invite you to find a few minutes to read it carefully. The wine-growers participated in the Rootstock Festival in Sydney, managing to blend perfectly what is the internationalization of the product associated with Italian culture, without receiving any institutional or financial support of the State. They have used those resources of Italians in the world that our institutions continue to ignore. I shall conclude, Mr President - and I would ask you to attach the full text of my speech to the proceedings - by saying that the involvement of Italians in the world not only makes life easier for us, but also enables us to act directly, effectively and efficiently to promote our services and products abroad, especially for small and medium-sized companies. Italians around the world are ready to play their part if Italy, and therefore the Government, institutions and the Italian public administration want to do so. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u317	Senator Moscardelli is registered to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u318	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, after the excellent illustrations made by my fellow Members in their capacity as rapporteurs, I think it would be useful to highlight some of the elements of this measure that have as their destination our production system and, therefore, the economic revival of our country, as well as to indicate some measures that we might have as our objective, which we cannot achieve here, given the long permanence of the decree in the other branch of Parliament. The development passes through research, energy, digital and export: this was how the Undersecretary De Vincenti expressed himself on "Il Sole 24 ORE" on 12 February, and I believe that this is a fair summary of the measure that we approve. The limited resources and the respect of budgetary constraints have certainly influenced the scale of the interventions with the aim of reconciling the essential requirements of stability of the public accounts with those relating to economic development. The guiding thread of all the rules are competitiveness and the determining factors, such as research, the cost of energy (even if we could widen the intervention and understand all the so-called energy companies), technological modernization and the internationalization of our companies. Of fundamental importance in order to allow our companies to recover the accumulated delay on this side are the recognition of the tax credit for the investments in research and the vouchers for the small and medium enterprises that purchase equipment of information communication technology, connect to broadband or develop solutions of e-commerce. The changes made during the debate in the Chamber of Deputies have made it possible to adjust some initial problems, for example in the area of land cover, since initially it was allowed only for the companies of the Mezzogiorno. With the resolution adopted at its meeting on 13 June last year in the Finance Committee on the annual report on the activity of Equitalia, we had put forward ten proposals to the Government, which were unanimously adopted there, to improve the relationship between citizens and taxpayers, in relation to the issue we were dealing with. Some measures have been immediately transposed by the Government into the so-called "doing" measure and another - of fundamental importance, such as the compensation of taxes with the credits certified to the public administration - has been transposed into what we are about to approve today. Further interventions, after those introduced in the approval of the law of stability on the credit side, increase the ability of our companies to find the financial resources necessary to support the production process. The rules on securitisation therefore extend to transactions carried out by subscription or purchase of financial bills and it is expected that the Central Guarantee Fund for small and medium-sized enterprises will also be able to present guarantees in favour of savings management companies which, on behalf of mutual funds, subscribe minibonds. To be emphasized is the measure that recognizes the possibility for young people and women to acquire zero-rate loans to start new businesses, with the extension also to the field of trade and tourism. Positive are other interventions on the reform of the condominium and on the topic of the certification of the energy performance. With the introduction of the voucher for small and medium-sized enterprises to support digitalisation and technological modernisation, our productive fabric will be able to start processes that increase its competitiveness. This measure, together with the tax credit for research, introduces a mix of key incentives for our companies. I also think it useful to point out some of the objectives that, as a Parliament, we can pursue with the next Government, because they are essential to try to get out of a condition of our economic situation as a zero comma improvement syndrome. In fact, there is a need to grasp fundamental elements in this 2014 in order to give a strong push that binds the economic recovery to the increase of the job, elements that I will remember only for titles. I would remind you of the significant lowering and reduction of the so-called improper burden on businesses, i.e. the contribution to unemployment, maternity, sickness and family allowances, without affecting the proportion of contributions which, on the other hand, finances pension funds and thus represents tomorrow's income for thousands of workers. I also recall the significant reduction in IRAP, especially through the extension of the deductibility of labour costs, which cannot be subject to taxation. I would like to take this opportunity to express here my regret at an amendment whereby, through the increase in taxation on financial transactions - excluding, of course, government bonds, white list bonds, long-term savings plans or postal vouchers - a significant cut in IRAP could have been covered (sin has been rejected). Similarly, I would like to recall the strengthening of the role of local self-government as actors in the development plan, especially in relation to the mountain of resources of Community funds 2014-2020. I also recall a revision of the stock options discipline, which is not fair to be subject to INPS contribution. We must work on companies, because, through economic recovery and work, we can support domestic demand, not having at the moment the mountain of resources necessary to increase incomes. Those who earn zero euros can earn a salary: this can be the way to support domestic demand as well, which is fundamental, since export alone is not enough. [[Applause from the PD Group and Senator Buemi.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u321	Mr President, Mr President-in-Office of the Government, ladies and gentlemen, my speech can only begin with the name of this measure: 'Destination Italy', but which Italy? There is no possibility of planning a future, there are too many uncertainties and hesitations and there is no incisive action and courage. There is no structural reform needed to implement that phase of growth and development for which this Government has so far committed itself only in words. And among these, the reasoning moves inevitably on the weight of the direct and indirect tax pressure, for which the measure does not foresee any decisive and necessary cut for the revival of a country that needs competitiveness, having to compare with reality to taxation of much lower and therefore putting our companies out of the market, making them not competitive. Another key area is research and development, for which a tax credit is set up for the companies investing in it, but the limited resources allocated are linked to 50% of the expenditure implemented by the companies themselves, which are often small and medium-sized, with modest possibilities for intervention - perhaps even smaller at this time - as well as being obviously a contribution subject to the approval of the European Commission: therefore, bureaucracy on bureaucracy. On the energy front, the cuts announced to component A3 of the electric bill will remain incomplete measures, as the rule for the application is uncertain and unlikely and the discounts will presumably be minimized. We are not talking about the new charges on the electricity system, resulting from the possible award of a tender to create a coal-fired thermal power plant in Sulcis, provided with a special section of the plant for the capture and storage of the production of carbon dioxide. In summary, we can say that this decree, whose declared aim should be to remedy the competitive disadvantage accumulated by Italy compared to the European countries and other advanced economies, does not bring internal courageous initiatives of change, that they see in the small and medium-sized enterprises and in this productive fabric - present mainly in the northern part of the country, that as I have said previously is left out of the measure under consideration - the starting point for the economic recovery. It is lacking in the resources allocated, limited and insufficient, but it is also lacking in the solutions proposed, which, as I have already said, do not touch on fundamental issues such as credit rationing and excessive and oppressive fiscal and bureaucratic burdens. We must also remember the passage that the North League strongly wanted in the Chamber of Deputies, with the abolition of Article 8, discriminatory and anti-competitive rule, which went against any kind of economic development and growth in the sector and not just the insurance sector. Some data from the EU countries put Germany first in R &amp; D investment with €74 billion invested at 2.8% of GDP, which accounts for almost 30% of the resources spent across the EU. Italy is in 16th place, with 19 billion euros, equal to 1.26 percent of GDP. While the data on the behaviour of the enterprises is ambivalent, since it shows trends with investments in decrease for the enterprises with many employees and trends in increase for the enterprises with few employees, the data on the public funds that Italy allocates for the research is clearly in decrease. The text that we are about to approve also tries to take on the specifics of the Italian reality in order to favor dynamics of growth of the micro-enterprises; in fact also the consortia and the networks of enterprises are favored in order to involve more the fabric of the SMEs. Even the 500 million turnover roof represents a clear calibration of the measure in the direction described. This is a first step: in many European countries the tax credit mechanism is structural and applies to all expenditure not only on the incremental part. Having said that, we often touch with hand how inferior the relationship of interaction between research centres and the business world is to other countries. There is a supply chain at the various stages of research, ranging from the theoretical and basic level to the actual technology transfer, which in Italy is not chained and efficient and on which it is necessary to intervene, also in order to make effective and real the opportunities that we give to companies with the actions provided for in the decree. A decisive issue is the cost of energy. The debate on cost components has been open for some time, and this decree certainly does not say the word end. The decree touches on some points also controversial, discussing on a voluntary basis the incentives on renewables. The coordination between the various companies and authorities acting with different but complementary competences will be important, so that the definition of certain parameters that seem to safeguard, such as the guaranteed minimum price, does not become a further penalising element. I would also like to highlight the action on the parafiscal components of gas, which are positively poured on companies that live in world competition. With regard to gas, I do not want to give the impression of speculating on it, but I believe that in the face of the tragic events that are taking place in Ukraine and the substantial disinterest they detect in the international community, choices made by this Parliament that diversify Italian supplies and make us free to stand alongside the peoples who remain subjugated for the strategic interests of other States. As regards the context conditions, I would like to point out the importance of the measures provided for in Article 4 concerning the re-industrialisation of sites of national interest. I would also like to point out the measures to strengthen the minibond instrument. This means activating and strengthening an alternative to banking financing channel. This too is one of the handicaps of our country, highlighted by the study centres and the OECD, where it is noted that the excessive dependence on the banking system is an element that limits the financing and growth capacities of companies. I would like to point out the measures to support the creation of micro and small young and female enterprises, that is to say to the spread of entrepreneurship, one of the strong points of our country. Finally, it is no coincidence that a significant part of article 13 is dedicated to Expo 2015, the acceleration and completion of the works for Expo. Expo 2015 will be a key moment in which Italy will meet technological innovations and will be a great showcase of the technological innovation capacity of the Italian economy. In conclusion, the Government considers that this measure is important in order to engage the recovery and strengthen the capacity of the Italian economy for economic recovery. I once again thank the senators of the two Commissions for their work these days and I hope that the Senate will convert this decree into law, which is strongly awaited by the companies, workers and social forces of our country. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u345	We shall now proceed to the examination of Article 1 of the draft law. I note that the amendments are intended to refer to the articles of the decree-law to be converted, in the text including the amendments made by the House of Deputies. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u346	Mr President, in our view, Article 1 unfortunately condemns our country to go back to the past in terms of clean sources, energy efficiency and incentives. When I talk about going back to the past, I point to the perspective offered by paragraphs 11 and 14, which in fact is to subsidise the oldest and most polluting sources, such as coal. The paragraphs from 11 to 14 included in fact, open the doors to the construction of a new coal-fired thermoelectric power plant to be built in Sulcis Iglesiente. I believe that we should have taken action on this area, and we should also have had a more forward-looking debate, so the reason for our amendments is to try to avoid this return to the past. Despite some changes made by the House of Deputies, which reduce the damage and the extent of Article 1 as a whole, some points remain unacceptable in our view, which I would like to stress: I am referring in particular to the retroactive reduction of incentives to renewable sources, because even a kind of blackmail is being done. This relates to paragraph 1, with regard to the issue of bio-prices, but I would like to highlight in particular the amendments that have been tabled to delete or reformulate paragraphs 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6. Since we have given up our general debate to focus on the substantive issues of the amendments, I would like to emphasise in particular that paragraphs 3 to 6 introduce these so-called "spam incentives" rules for renewables, through a mechanism that can be activated by voluntary renegotiating of facilities, extending the duration of seven years and reducing the annual amount. We clearly argue that, rather than a voluntary renegotiation, it is, in fact, a sort of blackmail. In fact, in essence, the possibility is offered to the owners of plants already in operation to voluntarily extend their remaining incentive period of seven years in exchange for a fair reduction of the value of the incentive perceived, but that is not fixed and is defined in a future measure. In fact, however, it is stated that the proposal is impossible to refuse. We talk about blackmail because those who will not accept the reduction and therefore choose to continue to enjoy the incentive regime, due for the remaining period of law, for a period of ten years, will not be able to access any other type of incentive (rejections, full enhancements). Therefore, the bill is diluted by very little, but - in our opinion - at the same time we continue in an oscillating policy that has characterized - alas! - the last years of our country, in which we started with the energy account and gave a strong boost to renewable energy and then gave a stop, but then changed again. The only thing you can't do in this area is change the rules of the game all the time, because no business, and I don't think any individual or family, can think of making any kind of investment if every two or three months you change the rules. For this reason, we have concentrated most of our amendments to remedy this further harassment, namely the change of rules, in particular by proposing the deletion of paragraphs 3 to 6 and by trying to reduce the damage.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u347	Mr President, with amendment 1.12 it is proposed to avoid unnecessary confusion of application, indicating the contractually relevant term "grant power" instead of "electric power." Amendment 1.13, which I signed, proposes to exclude from the application of the standard hydroelectric plants with a concession power of less than or equal to 1 megawatt, i.e. 1,000 kilowatts, instead of 500 kilowatts, as indicated in the basic text, in order to avoid a very significant impact on the budgets of small plants, i.e. installations in operation with particularly high operating costs. In this regard, I note the report of the Politecnico di Milano in support of Resolution No. 618 of 2013 of the Authority for Electricity and Gas. I also point out that, as the concession power and therefore the annual electricity production increase, today the support of the guaranteed minimum prices is already reduced, that the aforementioned resolution of the Authority has limited to 1.5 megawattora produced in the year compared to the previous 2 megawattora annual. Among other things, the impact of guaranteed minimum prices in favour of so-called mini-hydro plants on the A3 component is very limited. In essence, therefore, this amendment proposes to support the production of the main renewable source, which - as we know - is hydroelectric. Amendment 1.85 concerning the area of assessment of the reimbursement of gas distribution plants proposes the deletion of the first part of paragraph 16, which identifies new ways for the calculation of the reimbursement of gas distribution plants which the contracting operator of the first minimum territorial tendering procedures will have to recognize to the outgoing operator. It is considered, in fact, preferable to maintain the previous wording of article 15, paragraph 5, of Legislative Decree no. 164 of 2000, as it constitutes for almost 15 years a constant reference term for the evaluation of the value of the plants, to which they have been able to rely both the enterprises and the local authorities owners of the service. In order to ensure an orderly process of liberalisation of the sector, which will receive a strong impetus with the celebration of the upcoming competitions for the minimum territorial scope, it is in fact considered useful to maintain the stability of the regulatory framework, avoiding introducing additional elements of uncertainty, which would discourage participation in the tenders, with the risk of delaying the administrative process for the publication of the notices and feeding disputes between local authorities and companies. This is also in order to avoid likely appeals and therefore new elements of uncertainty proposed by the parties concerned with regard to different negotiation agreements, on which the reform would have an impact. Finally, since the valuation criteria provided for in the previous wording of Article 15 of Decree No. 164 of 2000 have been used for the capital assessment of installations in the context of extraordinary acquisition and merger transactions, their modification would have negative effects on the capital value of the companies that committed themselves to carrying out investments in this direction, potentially leading to the emergence of accounting and tax losses. The proposal for a measure which is called for to be repealed would contradict the industrial policy pursued so far, paradoxically penalising those companies which have cooperated in its pursuit. I am beginning to conclude the speech: given the difficulties that contracting stations are encountering in complying with the time limits laid down in the rules for the publication of notices, it is considered appropriate to maintain the second part of amendment 1,151, approved by the House of Deputies, which provides for an extension of the four-month deadline. If the Government and the rapporteur consider that they do not accept the amendment, I would ask that it be changed and accepted as an agenda.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u348	Mr President, I rise to explain Amendments 1.9 and 1.18. However, I would like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, Mrs Fabbri, and especially the rapporteur, Mr Olivero, for having mentioned our responsible opposition and our contribution to this measure in 10a and 6a in this House. Both of these amendments are deleted. In our opinion, paragraph 2 of the article has a negative impact on about 60,000 small producers, with a reduction in revenues, which in some cases even reaches 40% and with the consequent reduction of the relative tax revenue, compared to a benefit for the community that is really not significant and that translates, for the component "A3" into a saving of just 1.26 percent. More specifically, the savings for a small manufacturing company are around 59 cents (equivalent to 0.41 percent circus) and for a family, with consumption of 3,500 kilowatt hours per year, around 49 cents (equivalent to 0.27 percent of the bill). At the same time, we ask for the deletion of paragraph 3, because there is a high risk that the 700 million reduction of the component "A3" will really remain a theoretical and not real figure, causing - with the activation of the clause of the lack of access to the incentives for ten years, for any type of intervention realized on the plants that have not opted for the voluntary remodulation - a block of the investments on interventions of renovation and strengthening. So we risk not only not reaching the savings hoped for in the bill, but giving up all the positive spillovers in terms of induced, employment, tax and tax revenues, which could lead to such investments, which in the last ten years have involved about 1,000 plants, exceeding the total figure of 9 billion euros. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u353	Mr President, I would like to explain the amendments 1.66, deletion of paragraph 12 of Article 1 of the Decree-Law, and 1.82, addition of a new paragraph after paragraph 15. The first intends to cancel the evil idea of building a coal-fired power station in Sulcis. The reasons for this proposal are different. First of all, the power plant will not use coal from an existing mine, but from South America, Australia, Brazil, and this is because local coal produces non-desulfurable gas. So, if you want to make people think you're gonna use that coal, it's fair to know that this is fake. Moreover, it is very noble that we want to reduce the cost of electricity. But if we really want to achieve this, we should not encourage the production of a coal-fired power station with 30 cents per kilowatt hour (63 million taken from the bills). If we really want to reduce the cost of electricity, it is unthinkable to encourage the production of such a power station which also uses a fossil source. Thirdly, it is absolutely not clear where CO2 produced at the same time as using coal should be stored, and there is no such plan. Fourthly, this seems to us to be just a system to use the method of compensation to get money on the ground. If we really want money to arrive on the territory and really want to reduce the cost of the electricity bill, I would point out that some amendments propose with a very good sense to replace this project (which makes no sense) with less costly projects for the people. I therefore call on the Government not to say things and then to do others. Amendment 1.82 refers to the incentive criteria for the biofuels supply chain. This is fine, but we must pay attention to the places where we go to cultivate biomass that we want to use to produce biofuels. If, in fact, we do not make a good operation of the agricultural land, we subtract portions in order to devote the latter to the cultivation of biomass for the production of biofuels; in fact, where the cost of the electric bill is eventually reduced because it is produced biofuel on site, then we will end up importing food products and, therefore, what is saved on the one hand is lost on the other. Or, banally, if we decide to import already partially refined biofuels or virgin oils such as palm oil, we also need to be careful where these palms are grown. In Indonesia, in fact, the rainforest is being destroyed to cultivate palm trees whose oil production we, very furbly, would like to buy. I remember that all these crops overlap with agricultural crops: they need more water, more soil processing, more pre-plantation fertilizations, more fertilizations, more plant protection treatments, all things known and true. Therefore, if we want to encourage the production of biofuel, at least we encourage it with a minimum of criterion: otherwise we believe we save when instead we spend more in other ways. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u368	With regard to the agenda G1,100, the Government welcomes it by proposing a reformulation, because in reality the incentive system for renewable sources has already been defined and, therefore, it is not necessary to define it. For the rest, the first commitment remains unchanged, as well as the second commitment. The third commitment is reworded as follows: "to support the technological development of transmission networks." Finally, the fourth commitment remains unchanged.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u393	MARTELLI (M5S). Mr President, Amendment 1.12 correctly identifies a parameter, that of power, for hydroelectric installations, beyond which they are excluded from the scope of this paragraph. In this regard, I must say that it makes no sense to talk about electrical power, because it is not in any way related to the actual productivity of a plant and, therefore, to the volume of electrical current possibly outside the dedicated withdrawal. Much more correctly you should use the rated concession power. Therefore, it is more than sensible to replace the word "electric" with the other "grant" words. I remember that an electric power of 500 kilowatts can correspond to a nominal concession power of 300 or 250 kilowatts, because the calibration of the plant is a function of many parameters, such as the flow rate of water. So talking about electric power tout court doesn't make sense. We therefore call on the House to vote in favour of this amendment, to indicate more correctly that it is a concession power.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u406	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u408	GIROTTO (M5S). Mr President, Amendment 1.37 aims at abolishing the so-called interruption service, which currently provides for the possibility for some final customers, mainly metalworking companies, to obtain substantial economic premiums in view of their willingness to stop their activities and their electricity consumption on the basis of orders given by Terna, who is in charge of dispatching. It is a service designed for the safety of the electrical system, to which only users with high electricity consumption, introduced in 2004. Since 2010, the change in the conditions and factors at stake in the electricity system, characterised by a sharp contraction, i.e. a decrease in consumption, and a high increase in the electricity grid emissions from renewable energy sources, has meant that this service has already been exceeded, but contributions continue to burden the electricity bill. At present, in this period the supply of energy is able to satisfy and overcome the demand: this, through a complementarity between renewable and traditional sources. Therefore, this would make the interruptability service superfluous, at least in its current formulation. The important thing is that this amendment does not create greater burdens for the state but, on the contrary, it saves about EUR 700 million per year, which at this time are burdened on electricity bills for users in general and especially for SMEs and those 60,000 craftsmen who were in the square yesterday, as another colleague also recalled. In short, with Amendment No 1,37 we propose EUR 700 million in savings.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u415	Milo (GAL). Mr President, the provision for our examination, in Article 1 (7), replaces paragraphs 3 and 3- bis of Article 6 of Legislative Decree No 192 of 2005 on energy performance certificates, abolishing the obligation to allegation from acts of transfer of buildings free of charge and from leases. At the same time, Article 5, paragraph 2, of Decree-Law No 151 of 2013, the so-called milleproroghe, which returned yesterday from the Chamber, intervening on the energy performance certificate, provides for the disapplication of paragraph 3-a of Article 6 of Decree-Law No 192. Therefore, there is clearly a conflict in this dual legislation. If we do not approve of amendment 1.51, we will find ourselves with the rule of this decree and the rule of the thousand-pronge between them conflicting. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u464	BLUNDO (M5S). Mr President, I would add my signature to Amendment 1.84. The standard amends the rules for the assessment of natural gas distribution networks in cases where the granting acts or agreements do not specifically provide for the criteria to be used for the determination of the value of natural gas for the purposes of the concession. With this amendment we anticipate the equivalence between values. The provision in the decree-law in fact determines a difference of treatment between the operators because it only affects the valorization of some networks that in almost all the cases are those of the public companies. This also has a negative impact on the administration's budgets and makes the purchase of these networks extremely attractive. The rule, of considerable scope, also intervenes a few weeks after the expiry of the tenders relating to the first two groups of ATEM and could question the refund values already defined, thus leading to severe economic imbalances, in addition to the impossibility to ban the tender. I would ask you to reflect on this amendment because it is necessary to reformulate the provision by removing the use of RAB (regulatory base asset), which is limited in any case to particular cases, by providing for the transfer from the VIR (residual industrial value) of private attachment contributions, in order to determine a first alignment of the two values.

We are convinced that the abolition of the minimal INPS contribution can free the desire to do business in Italy, help the economic recovery of our country and obtain more income in the state coffers thanks to the increase of entrepreneurial activities. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Conclude, Senator Bencini. BENCINI (M5S). It is also a zero-cost reform, as lower contributions correspond to lower future pension payments. The imposition of a minimum contribution is a major obstacle to the birth or continuation of small businesses. If we want our economy to become more modern and open, we must abolish the minimal INPS for small businesses and replace it with payments proportional to revenue. We would have liked to have discussed this in the Labour and Social Security Committee, of which I am a member. [[Brusio. References by the President]] , in a homogeneous and constitutional measure devoted exclusively to the revival of the economy of small businesses. As the authors do not insist on the vote, the agendas for G3,102 and G3,103 are accepted as a recommendation. Amendment 3.0.1 is not possible. In view of the contrary opinion expressed by the 5th Commission under Article 81 of the Constitution, amendments 3.0.3 and 3.0.4 are impervious. We shall now proceed to the examination of the amendments to Article 4 of the Decree-Law, which I invite the authors to explain.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u530	Mr President, Article 4 is very important, although unfortunately in a negative way. In fact, despite some changes that have been made by the House, thanks to a mobilization and an information campaign, in fact, this article intervenes with changes on Article 252- bis of the Code of the Environment and, in fact, in some way largely derogates from the principle that those who pollute pay. In the original article 4 of the decree there was a real pardon that concerned those who signed the program agreement. For those responsible for pollution, and we know very well that there are many managers in the national industrial sites (SIN) to be reclaimed, it was found the escamotage to extend the reclamation itself to those who signed the programme agreement. Despite the changes that have been made by the House, we believe that this article should be completely removed from the decree at our examination. So our amendments - and, fortunately, many other amendments - are in this sense: they are deleted, like Amendment 4.1, or they are amendments that make it clearer who has to pay, who is responsible for pollution and, therefore, for the payment of all the reclamation works. We also address, once again, some issues, always within the framework of the reclamations of thousands of sites, but we know that there are still thousands of polluted sites that have remained without master, in the sense that they are related to failed companies or other; this issue has not been dealt with at all by the Government. All our amendments are therefore aimed at eliminating Article 4 or at trying to improve and reduce the damage and at restoring the polluter pays principle.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u782	ZELLER (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). (IT) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, in order to approve the Decree-Law Destinazione Italia in good time, thus avoiding its decadence, it was not possible for the Senate to make further improvements. However, the text on which we are to speak today contains some improvements introduced to the House of Deputies, which I would like to mention briefly here. I am referring in particular to the amendment of Article 10 by our colleagues from linguistic minorities in the House, where we have finally managed to establish the so-called business court in Bolzano, a section of the business court. This will guarantee the right enshrined in our Statute of autonomy to the use of its mother tongue before the court, with bilingual trials, therefore not only in Italian, but also in German. And thanks to the work of colleagues from Trentino to the Chamber was also saved the seat of the court of the companies of Trento, which the decree provided to assemble to the district of Venice. A solution has therefore been found which will benefit the entire Trentino-Alto Adige/Südtirol region. We also welcome the removal from the RC regulations that had so much to discuss. The trade associations had rightly raised objections to the formulation of anti-fraud rules which provided, for example, for compensation in a specific form at approved bodies and for the provision of medical and health services rendered by professionals affiliated with insurance undertakings. In this original version of the decree we saw the risk of a limitation of the freedom of choice by the citizens together with a limitation of the freedom of enterprise. Finally, we welcome the corrections on the energy certification of buildings, which resolve the question of the nullity of the contracts of sale and lease determined precisely by the failure to submit the declaration of energy efficiency. With the provision of a financial penalty, we believe that a fair and legal solution has been found. The measure as a whole includes a number of positive measures aimed at relaunching the competitiveness of businesses, especially those in support of small and medium-sized enterprises and those in favour of female entrepreneurship, but these are measures - as previous speakers have already said - which are obviously not sufficient to relaunch the country's economy. These are the first steps, as we believe that far more incisive and reductive measures are needed in order to restore the economy than the bureaucracy that is strangling the country. Well, this is a small "Destinazione Italia" (Destination Italy), a time of departure. Certainly there are some good things, but right now I think that Italy does not need any further rules, many new rules, but it would need to simplify those that are already there and direct all the resources of the country towards certain objectives, those that serve to make it restart. The incentives for women and small businesses are fine, because they are the backbone of the country, but we must ensure that certainties are not only there when the enterprise starts, but also in the entrepreneurial path. Certainties of the times and certainties of a vision of government and a governance that the country needs, because only in this way can we give certainty to companies and, therefore, to the future of our companies. The vote of the Per l'Italia Group will be in favour, because we think of this as a starting point, with the hope that the new Government, whose confidence we will vote soon in this House, can really rethink this objective and have a true vision of our great country.
