ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u786	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u787	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u788	DE PETRIS (Misto-SEL). Mr President, this decree, with a somewhat presumptuous title ('Destinazione Italia'), is once again - alas - a sort of omnibus measure which includes a series of interventions that are often not homogeneous: on the contrary, once again it is dissimilar rules, in violation of the indications, also referred to in the last letter of President Napolitano, Article 77 of the Constitution. What is the political point, President? With measures of this nature, which indicate, as in this case, Italy's objectives, namely to ensure that companies, even from abroad, come to invest in Italy, in reality, do not pursue these objectives in a clear and precise way, but try to put in place a series of measures that are often not coordinated with each other. What is the political problem in the face of such unevenness? Undoubtedly, a number of amendments adopted to the House (I can only talk about the transition to the House, because, as usual, here in the Senate, we did not have the opportunity to make any changes) have made some improvements that I also mentioned during the debate: a reduction of the damage in some cases, but also included rules, for example on female entrepreneurship, which we are pleased with. However, a section on insurance has been released from the text. In our view, it would certainly have been important to achieve a reduction in spending on citizens. As you can see, when you want to expunge, expunge. Then there is the question of the reading voucher, which in the end has been very reduced, although on this point the Letta Government had set up a media campaign. Measures remain to enable foreign companies to arrive. This leads us to make a point: we would have also wanted a series of measures to ensure that the multinationals already present in Italy do not leave. We still don't know what's going to happen to Electrolux; we know - alas - what's happening to Fiat. In this decree Destinazione Italia we would have at least satisfied ourselves with some clear and precise measures to ensure that some companies remain in our country. There are some points that we consider absolutely negative: I would like to refer them briefly. First of all, there is the energy part, which, as usual, was intended to reduce in some way the prices for households and businesses. This will result in a real risk of rising prices for Italian households that have not joined the free market (and it is good to know). On the other hand, once again the choices that are made with this decree, with the interventions provided for in Article 1, in our opinion condemn our country to return to a past that we thought should never happen again. I am referring to clean sources, energy efficiency and incentives. Once again incentives are granted to subsidise the oldest and most polluting sources, such as coal. I am not going back to the question of Sulcis, on which we have already spoken in the context of the examination of the amendments. Overall, EUR 1.2 billion is made available for the benefit of the most polluting sources responsible for climate change. These subsidies are paid by all Italians through bills, contradicting the purpose of the measure, which was to reduce the cost of energy. Among other things, the instrument of certification of energy performance is weakened, and the mechanism of retroactive cutting on renewable sources is unacceptable for us. The political point is that we cannot continue to change the rules and rules. I really very much hope that the new government will choose once and for all what the national energy strategy will be, which in our opinion can only be to focus not only generically on diversification, but above all on clean and renewable energy sources; this would really be good for the country. Another point - although, I repeat, has been modified with a reduction in damage thanks to the interventions adopted by the House and the media campaign that there was - concerns Article 4, i.e. the reclamation of sites of national interest. Despite the changes, we believe that this legislation should have been further improved. In particular, the rules could be better specified in order to avoid loopholes for those responsible for pollution, in derogation from the principle that polluters pay. We have proposed a very clear amendment, which provided that the programme agreement was not of a transactive nature and that the obligation to repair damage caused by pollution should remain in place. Several amendments had been tabled by many senators; the Government could at least have accepted an agenda to ensure that the implementation of the interventions was truly directed to the safety, clean-up and repair of environmental damage and that contributions could not be attributed to those who had caused the damage itself. There remains a problem about the reclamations - I repeat - that Article 4 does not address, that is, how to clean up the thousands of polluted orphan sites, that is that they have remained without master, without the responsible ones: perhaps the companies have failed and no responsibility has been identified. This happens, among other things, in territories subject to illegal trafficking in special and dangerous waste. Therefore, it would have been important - and I am addressing the Secretary of State - to accept the proposal to set up a national fund on the model of the American superfund, which is supported by the business world, in proportion to the danger and environmental impact caused by the specific production sector. These vulnus remain, which for us are certainly not secondary, in addition to all the shortcomings I have mentioned before. For this reason the Senators of the Mixed Left Group Ecology and Freedom will vote against the measure.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u789	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u790	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u791	COUNCIL (LN-Aut). Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, in this House we have discussed many of these measures this year; some very important, others a little less. Many of these were united by a double thread in both form and substance. In the form, many of these were decree-laws. All decrees should be marked by urgency and consistency of content. I would like to make this clear, Madam President, because, during my speech, I will be curious to understand, together with the Government - which Government I do not know - what can be combined with the provisions contained in them, since they range from credit, to business, to measures for Expo. The second common thread is the name. After the famous decree of doing (which we have not quite understood what it did), after the decree saves Rome, which will see its new debut tomorrow in this Chamber, determining for the fate of the City Hall of Capitoline but sank a first time, a few weeks ago, even from a sentence of the same Hall of Montecitorio, we arrived at Destination Italy, a very challenging name (now the names are very roboating). That name is undoubtedly challenging, Madam President. The title made us hope to find ourselves faced with a measure of such a magnitude to change the fate of this evil country, but we are still far from the mocking decree-law n. 201 of 2011, known as save Italy. It's been a few years but nothing saved, in fact, things have always gotten worse. But without a doubt, ladies and gentlemen, this measure also devotes little to this country, to this Italy. A country - it should be remembered - that continues to mark indicators of heavy negativity in economic terms: the constantly stagnating GDP, a level of unemployment that has now broken down among young people 40% (absolute record), while every day we witness the suicide of an entrepreneur, and we should certainly take this into account. If, therefore, the answer to these dramas that are becoming daily in Italy is the measure Destinazione Italia, then the goal of our young people, Madam President, can only be abroad: you can only emigrate. Compared to the competitive relaunch of the Italian production system, in fact, the choice of the instrument of the decree-law is already inappropriate, which does not allow to carry out system reforms precisely because of its nature of act directed to provide for situations of exceptional urgency and necessity. The disastrous economic situation in Italy does not seem to us to be a question of the last few days and, therefore, it is perhaps wrong to speak of an urgent decree. If we want to relaunch the country, you must present a real plan of productive revival. We have always had in this House, in our speeches, the regret that there was no real industrial programme. Today's provision is a set of uncourageable dispositions: some that can be shared, were nothing other than for the laudable purpose, but too reductive with respect to the purpose of the pompous name by which we know this decree. That this was not a decree born under a good star had already been understood by how the question on the now famous article 8 had been handled, strongly wanted by the oppositions, and especially by the North League which, from the beginning, opposed an absolutely discriminatory and anticompetitive rule, which had the sole purpose of favoring insurance companies, without any benefit to the users nor much less for an economic category, such as that of the coachmen, mistreated in a bad way by the late articulated. But it must be said, Madam President, that we will also try to test the pulse of the majority who, although they are destined to change the Government and the President of the Council, still remain the majority. In fact, we understand that a draft law has been submitted to the Secretary of State Vicari. We will try to understand everything that has been extracted from Article 8 what will happen to our coachmen. We will see the position of the majority. In a decree such as Destinazione Italia one could not do better than a provision as unfair as the one suppressed; the coachmen will be grateful. However, dear coachmen, do not sleep quietly. We are in fact curious to read that bill. We consider intervention in favour of businesses to be restrictive. This is a great tragedy, Madam President. We cannot fail to appreciate the attempt made under Article 3, which establishes a tax credit for companies investing in research and development. Of course, EUR 600 million over three years is very few, Madam President, with resources from the next 2014-2020 programming of the Community Structural Funds: few, very few. It is clear that a competitive gap such as the Italian one cannot be exceeded with only 600 million euros. You will not really think that 600 million is enough to hold the pace of German, Indian or Chinese battleships: that is certainly not what we imagine, Madam President. All the more so because most of these resources are likely to end up only in certain regions and to me, who are part of the League group, comes from saying "the usual regions." However, we had asked to specify that a fair territorial division was guaranteed, because all regions have enormous difficulties in this period. It was an unnecessary exercise, Madam President. We have also pointed out that a tax credit equal to 50% of the incremental expenses, incurred by companies compared to the previous year, with a minimum expenditure of 50,000 euros in research and development, risked to exclude from the benefit a large part of the Italian companies, which are, by history, typically small and medium, characterized by a business size with a limited number of employees and a turnover that, when it goes well, comes to the order of a few million euros. In these companies an investment of 50,000 euros per year in the field of research and development is excessive and not consistent with the Italian production reality. The risk is that only a limited number of companies will be able to benefit from this provision. It would also be important to understand in concrete terms the question we had put about the deductibility of the hated Imu tax on instrumental properties. Reduce the tax wedge or lower the cost of labour: little, Madam President, in this measure. They are boulders, however, increasingly unsustainable, that are destroying our productive fabric. The proof of what I am saying you have had in these days, with the thousands of artisans and small entrepreneurs coming to the square in Rome yesterday, coming mainly from the North, to demonstrate against the policies of the Government and against an increasingly unsustainable tax pressure. Last night on television I saw the interview with a coachman, who would really be one of the most clicked on google at this time. In Piazza del Popolo there were 60,000 accredited people, people who work and make available all their time for their activity. It is a good thing, Mr President, that none of us has presented this measure, because we would have seen some good ones: woe if it had been presented to those workers! In your opinion, is the Destinazione Italia decree really a sufficient response to their requests? We have never imagined this since the first readings that were done in the House. Even today we have really seen and touched with hand the impossibility of putting the Senate in a position to ensure that even one small amendment was touched. The only thing we are sure of, Madam President, is that it is a classification of provisions always in favour of friends: we cannot find other words to comment on this measure, so much so that tomorrow we will be in the Chamber the decree save Rome, but then there will be the saving Naples and the saving Reggio Calabria. [[Applause from Senator Arrigoni.]] And the other 8,000 municipalities who will save them? We have also asked for a small reduction in the pressure on the notorious Stability Pact: I can tell you, Madam President, that this measure is not so much a 'Destination Italy', but it looks more like a 'Destination Medjugorje', where I hope that as soon as possible we will all go together on a bus. [[Applause from the LN-Aut Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u794	MANCUSE (NCD). Mr President, at the moment, especially in this part of the hemicycle, some adjectives are in fashion, such as "useful" and "useless": well, I believe that this decree has arrived at the Senate in time to be approved, but it is probably useless to be modified, deepened and discussed. Our activity has therefore limited itself to the production of a copious agenda that I hope the future Government can take into due consideration. However, this is an effort: we start from the awareness that this decree will certainly not raise the fate of our country's economy, but we will need more decisive and courageous structural interventions. The decree Destinazione Italia contains a series of useful measures, which I will not list for brevity, which will allow the world of small and medium-sized enterprises to take a small breath of oxygen, just to avoid the collapse for respiratory arrest. These are measures in the energy sector, for companies, in the field of communication and reclamation interventions to promote Expo 2015, which will certainly help to promote the competitiveness of Italian companies. We know how much President Letta has promoted this decree in the world, and I must also say with some success. It has been the subject of constant attention from the former President of the Council, who has been spent on repeated occasions and in various world fora to promote the project contained within the plan, with a view to a laudable attempt at internationalization, to promote in a organic and structural way the attention and attraction of foreign investments and to promote, therefore, the competitiveness of Italian companies. In the end, this also means wanting to attract financial and human capital, giving rise to a new and robust demand in Italy. We know, however, that it would take more to get out of this very long crisis, which has lasted for more than six years; it is also true, however, that we begin to glimpse the first faint signs of a slight recovery. This evening, after so many voting efforts, the hoped-for approval of this measure, in the light of the political events that are taking place in the last few hours, will mean if the President-in-Office of the Council, Renzi, will be able to build a strong understanding in absolute transparency. Never, as at this time, do the Italians need clarity on a shared government programme, which has as its priority some fundamental reforms and structural measures necessary to restart the Italy system. In this case, the measure under consideration can provide aid and makes sense in the new Government's programme; otherwise, even if it is useful, it would be little significant because it ends in itself. We in the NCD Group vote in favour of this measure, at a time of economic, political and institutional difficulties, but at the same time we send a clear and strong message to the President-in-Office of Renzi and to the nascent Government. At this moment, Italy and the business world are a serious sick person, to whom we are giving aspirin. I'm a doctor and I know aspirin hardly hurts, even if it has some contraindications. In this case it doesn't have any, but with aspirin we certainly do not heal the serious patient: certainly more substantial measures are needed, more useful injections and therapies are needed to restart Italy and the system of companies in our country. [[Applause from the NCD Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u797	CASTALDI (M5S). Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, when it was presented (there was still the Letta Government and it was the month of September 2013), the Destinazione Italia plan was described (it is still on the web and, therefore, it is visible) as the Government's organic policy to attract foreign investment and promote the competitiveness of Italian companies. It's a show! A plan consisting, in the first version, of 50 measures that affect the entire life cycle of the investment and affect many areas: tax, work, civil justice, research, strengthening of the foreign network and improving the reputation of Italy abroad. Since then, there has been a certain thing: the government that proposed it and the President who had gloryed in it today are no longer there. We must ask ourselves, therefore, a first question: in your opinion, has Italy's reputation abroad improved? I'd say no. The distinctive emphasis of the value "Destination Italy" reaches its climax when the value of the plan is thus identified. On the other hand, in order to make Italy attractive, it is necessary to enhance its uniqueness and, therefore, its distinctive assets: beauty, culture, quality of life, diversity of our territories, ability to do business and create knowledge, propensity to innovation, rich human relations. In one expression, it is made in Italy. Decree-Law No 145 of 2013 has a very wide and complex content, as it affects a wide range of regulatory sectors and has extremely varied measures, inconsistent with the mission assigned to the decree and that the preamble qualifies them, in order to bring them back to a unified ratio, as essential factors for progress and opportunities for economic, cultural and civil enrichment and, at the same time, to relaunch the competitiveness of enterprises. We have understood, examining the decree, the strong interest of the capitals coming from Singapore, the strong attraction, the strong urgency to modify paragraph 9 of Article 1 on the norms of the civil code relative to the discipline of the condominium of the buildings, already recently novelized by the law of reform n. 220 of 2012. We have understood why the Kuwaiti sovereign fund has promised EUR 500 million as an interest in Italy: to compensate for the exclusion from the registration requirement of trolleys (type of operating machinery) when they travel on the road for short and occasional journeys, either empty or dependent, or to allow the hire of drivers for the transport of persons even with cars. And how not to recognize the urgency, always in order to improve the appeal of our country and its attractiveness, to modify the annexes of Legislative Decree no.152 of 2006 (which we commonly call the environmental code), in order to exclude from the number of works submitted to environmental assessment of the competence of the State and the Regions (listed in Annex II and Annex III to the second part of that Code) those destined to retain, regulate or accumulate the water in a lasting way, if represented by physical containment interventions aimed at the safety of polluted sites. Other than "Destinazione Italia" (Italian Destination) The measure lacks measures capable of stimulating growth, but it is equipped with appropriate grimaldelli that confuse the opinion of citizens, as in the so-called "game of the three cards": a trick that has many losers, who are called "citizens," and many winners, who are called: "special interests." We wanted to change - as we did in the Chamber of Deputies, as the 5 Star Movement Group, the political force that stands on the side of the citizens, in part succeeding - yet another measure that unites and collects disparate measures that are not only attractive or finalized in this sense, but also satisfying the usual, damned interests. We have been proposed an unacceptable take or leave. We leave you the responsibility for yet another rain of funding on Expo 2015 in Milan. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] We leave you, with pleasure, the responsibility that the State must compensate the companies that suffer damage in the yards of the so-called strategic infrastructures (Shibona Senator!): compensation based on the entirely indisputable presumption that the purpose of the damage is to hinder the yard itself (TAV!). We leave you with the responsibility to propose the securitisation system again (a tool that just a few years ago triggered a serious financial crisis, the negative effects of which are still being paid today: we have talked about it in its amendment), with an aggravating factor: these instruments will be allowed to even insinuate themselves in insurance guarantee funds, in the securities portfolios of social security institutions also public and currently, up to 50%, in pension funds. These are, and I repeat, instruments for which no financial rating obligation has even been foreseen, which have already caused catastrophic results: a time bomb placed in the national financial sector, which would need more security and stability. "We must lower the cost of the bills" was one of the "subra" that accompanied the Zen Government of Letta, from the first day, repeated again on this occasion [[Applause from the M5S Group]] : an operation that the Movement has managed in part to calmere, hindering the ambush, dictated by the great producers, that would have given the possibility to the private electric groups to take control of the Italian Stock Exchange with the objective, privatising it, to manage it to own advantage from the economic point of view that of the development of strategies to detriment of the small companies. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]] You have tried, knowing that there are costs for citizens in the measure, to compromise the renewable energy sector by abolishing the minimum guaranteed prices and applying the spread-incentive. But it wasn't enough, it wasn't enough: your arrogance went further, inserting paragraph 6-a into the Chamber in article 1, in which is recognized a discount on gas to energy companies, as always at the expense of small ones. To all this we must add the 60 million euros - I repeat: 60 million euros - that will be guaranteed from the first to the twentieth year of operation for the coal plant of Sulcis in Sardinia [[Applause from the M5S Group),]] already under Europe's lens for receiving public support of EUR 405 million from 1998 to 2010. Who will pay these costs? Foreign investors? I repeat as stated in the text: "The resulting charges (...) are borne by the Italian electricity system and they are paid by means of a corresponding levy on electricity tariffs". We translate it into language for citizens: yet another cost increase for consumers. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] The news from the Brussels report "For a European rebirth" in recent days indicates that Italian production lost around 24% between 2008 and 2013, a result that, besides including us among the countries with the worst performance, gives us a reflection: what can be the future of our country starting from a new policy proposal that places the work and the future of the young generations at the centre, as a priority? A rational and logical choice, easy to apply, but contrary to the interests of the big lobby (damned big lobby), there has been and we fear it will be difficult to support: to aim for the relaunch of the green economy, starting precisely from the energy conversion of the fossil system and supporting primarily energy efficiency. But you don't care about these things. I shall conclude, Madam President. You think to please the Italian lobbying organizations when the Secretary of State of one of the countries we would like to invest in Italy, the American Secretary of State Kerry, declares that climate change has become the world's greatest weapon of mass destruction, worse than nuclear proliferation or terrorism. The result of your decisions, which will always find the firm opposition of the citizens who have come to Parliament to defend themselves, is before everyone's eyes. [[Applause from the M5S Group]] Continue to exchange chairs and scripts without ever changing, without giving a change, with changes of pace or as you want to call them, closing your eyes and closing your ears to never really face the problems of the country. For all these reasons, I would like to announce, of course, the Italian citizens' vote against. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

This is an intervention which has been greatly improved during the examination of the Chamber of Deputies and which we would probably have improved further. There is no condoning in that article, while the principle to which we cannot fail, that of the polluter pays, is enshrined in it and reaffirmed. It is an article that establishes the conditions for which finally, in our country, a heritage of industrial realities, blocked to date because they have not benefited from any intervention of environmental rehabilitation and reclamation, can live a season of environmental recovery first and reindustrialization and industrial conversion then. We are very confident that this intervention, in particular, can also act as a flywheel for the resumption of useful investments in our country. I would therefore like to see a ban on any doubts and misunderstandings about a misinterpretation of this rule. In conclusion, Madam President, I welcome the work done. It is a useful and concrete measure - I repeat - directed to intercept the needs that come out of the country, from the real country, made of companies and families, forcefully. A recovery is possible and with this measure, it is probably even closer. That is why the Democratic Party Group will vote in favour of converting the decree into law. ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u815	FAVERO (PD). Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to talk to you about a project for complete integration - the LIS project in Cossato - which is at risk because of the lack of resources for bilingualism in the Italian-language sign language. We would like to guarantee the continuation of the project of bilingualism Italian language-sign language, carried out since 1994 - it is a unique project - by the Institute including Cossato, in the province of Biella, for the integration of deaf children, starting from the common school of childhood to continue in primary and secondary level. The project is at risk due to lack of funds, also due to particular situations determined in the province of Biella, which is commissioner and is in disarray, to which the Piedmont Region has also contributed after the cancellation of the 2010 elections. In this state of emergency, the resources made available by our Savings Bank Foundation, by the CRT, allow salaries to be paid to sign language interpreters only until March. The project currently covers 17 deaf students, twelve of whom in compulsory schools and five in high schools, of whom four are enrolled at the Institute of Higher Education in Cossathese and Valle Strona and one at the Professional Institute for Hotel and Catering in Trivero. But the project involves 168 children in compulsory school and 95, because there the integration takes place between normodates and deaf. These are students who have learned sign language early as a natural language, along with deaf students who use LIS as a second language and are therefore completely included and integrated. The lack of sign language interpreters would lead to a violation of the right to study enshrined in the Constitution, in particular for those children enrolled in the fifth year of high school who are going to undergo the exam of maturity and who, therefore, in the last three months of school will not have the opportunity to be helped by an interpreter and will therefore face maturity in his absence and without educators. That is why we have tabled a question asking the Minister to intervene, finally to equip the Institute of Cossay with the resources necessary to continue bilingualism, this beautiful project whose validity has also been recognized by the CNR. Once again the truth comes from Switzerland, like the investiture of Monti in 2011, like the pressures on Fabrizio Barca, born in the villa of Sankt Moritz by De Benedetti, card number one of the PD: all precooked, like a wurstel. And if anyone still believes in Italian newspapers, I ask: do you have news from the markets? They did not move an eyebrow: the Stock Exchange is stable, the spread sleepy. So, paraphrasing Shakespeare, so much noise for nothing. Renzi is the nothing that advances: another deaf ear to say the same things to. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2014-02-19-LEG17-Senato-sed-195.ana.u821	CANDIANI (LN-Aut). Madam President, thank you for your time. I want to bring to the attention of the House, and to anticipate a parliamentary question that I will address to the new Government and the new Minister for Economic Affairs, a resounding fact reported today by the daily newspaper "La Provincia di Varese" (The Province of Varese). Those who have subscribed good postal fruits thirty years before 1986, going to withdraw their subscription, are found with yields almost halved compared to the original title undersigned. We have thus discovered that in 1986 there was a decree by the Treasury Minister of the time that unilaterally modified returns. I believe that this is an absolute circumstance to be deepened, because postal savings are the farther away than speculative investment. The postal savings has always been the main refuge for the families of this country, together with the coupons and certificates of the Treasury. It is an attitude that is not acceptable and certainly to be deepened, because it would be really serious if after thirty years the families were to find themselves with nothing in hand compared to the forecast of original subscription, having given confidence to the Post Office that at the time took the money.
